, there exists
T=T(E^{\rm in},\theta)>0
such that

(3.126)

\int_{T}^{+\infty}(e^{-c_{0}t}C(E^{\rm in})+C)D(t)\,dt\leq(1-\theta)c_{0}\,.

Then the differential inequality becomes

(3.127)

\frac{dD}{dt}\leq-\theta c_{0}D+B\qquad\forall t\geq T\,,

where
B=e^{-c_{0}t}C(E^{\rm in})D+(C+c_{0})D
. Then Grönwall’s inequality will yield exponential convergence with rate
e^{-\theta c_{0}t}
, since the contribution of the
B
term in Duhamel’s formula is estimated by

(3.128)

\int_{T}^{t}e^{-\theta c_{0}(t-t^{\prime})}C(E^{\rm in})e^{-\theta^{\prime}c_{0}t^{\prime}}\underbrace{D(t^{\prime})e^{\theta^{\prime}c_{0}t^{\prime}}}_{\in L^{1}_{t^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\,dt^{\prime}\qquad\forall t\geq T\,,

where we choose
\theta^{\prime}\in(\theta,1)
. ∎

Finally, we conclude the convergence to equilibrium in Theorem
1.1
.

Proposition 3.8
.

If
\bm{X}
is a finite-energy solution to (
1.1
), then
\bm{X}
converges to an Euler elastica
\overline{\bm{X}}
in
H^{4}_{s}
as
t\to+\infty
.

Proof.

Free end
. We have already demonstrated that
\bm{X}_{ss}
is converging exponentially to zero in
H^{2}_{s}
. We should moreover demonstrate that
\bm{X}
is stabilizing as
t\to+\infty
. We integrate
\partial_{t}\bm{X}
from
t=t_{0}
to
t_{1}
and estimate in
L^{2}_{s}
:

(3.129)

\|\bm{X}(\cdot,t_{0})-\bm{X}(\cdot,t_{1})\|_{L^{2}_{s}}\lesssim\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}D^{1/2}(t)\,dt\,,\quad\forall t_{1}\geq t_{0}\geq 0\,.

Since
D(t)
has integrated exponential decay (
3.93
), we
obtain that
\bm{X}
is stabilizing exponentially in
L^{2}_{s}
as
t\to+\infty
.
Hence,
\bm{X}
is converging in
H^{4}_{s}
as
t\to+\infty
.

Closed loop
. In the periodic setting, we require the Lojasiewicz inequality in Proposition
6.1
: For every equilibrium
\overline{\bm{X}}
, there exists
\delta>0
,
\beta\in(0,1/2)
, and
A>0
such that if
\bm{X}\in H^{4}_{s}
is an inextensible curve satisfying
\|\bm{X}-\overline{\bm{X}}\|_{H^{4}_{s}}\leq\delta
, then

(3.130)

(E[\bm{X}]-E[\overline{\bm{X}}])^{1-\beta}\leq A\sqrt{D[\bm{X}]}\,.

The application of Lojasiewicz inequalities to geometric flows has a rich history starting with
[
98
]
. Our presentation is inspired by
[
51
, Chapter 2]
.

By the energy and propagation estimates,

(3.131)

E(t)\leq E^{\rm in}\,,\quad\lim_{t\to+\infty}D(t)=0\,,

which control
\bm{X}_{ss}
but not the center of mass
\int_{\mathbb{T}}\bm{X}(s,t)\,ds
. Define

(3.132)

\widetilde{\bm{X}}(s,t)=\bm{X}(s,t)-\int_{\mathbb{T}}\bm{X}(s,t)\,ds

and observe that
E[\widetilde{\bm{X}}]=E[\bm{X}]
and
D[\widetilde{\bm{X}}]=D[\bm{X}]
.

First, we demonstrate that
any sequence
\widetilde{\bm{X}}(s,t_{k})
with
t_{k}\to+\infty
contains a subsequence (not relabeled) converging in
H^{4}_{s}
. By weak compactness in
H^{4}_{s}
and the Rellich-Kondrachov theorem, there exists a subsequence satisfying

(3.133)

\widetilde{\bm{X}}(t_{k})\to\overline{\bm{X}}\text{ in }H^{4-\varepsilon}_{s}\,,\;\forall\varepsilon\in(0,4]\,,\qquad\widetilde{\bm{X}}(t_{k})\rightharpoonup\overline{\bm{X}}\text{ in }H^{4}_{s}

(3.134)

\lambda[\widetilde{\bm{X}}]\to\lambda[\overline{\bm{X}}]\text{ in }H^{1}_{s}\,.

Since
(\mathbf{I}+\gamma\bm{X}_{s}\otimes\bm{X}_{s})\bm{Z}_{s}
is weakly continuous in
L^{2}
with respect to the above convergences,
\overline{\bm{X}}
is a steady state:

(3.135)

D[\overline{\bm{X}}]=0\,.

Once we demonstrate that

(3.136)

\|\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{ssss}(\cdot,t_{k})\|_{L^{2}_{s}}\to\|\overline{\bm{X}}_{ssss}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}\,,

then we automatically have
\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{ssss}(\cdot,t_{k})\to\overline{\bm{X}}
strongly in
L^{2}_{s}
by the properties of weak convergence. For this, we observe that since
D[\widetilde{\bm{X}}]\to 0=D[\overline{\bm{X}}]
and
(\lambda[\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{s}]\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{s}+\kappa_{3}\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{s}\times\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{ss})_{s}
converges strongly in
L^{2}
, we must have (
3.136
); ergo,
\widetilde{\bm{X}}(\cdot,t_{k})\to\overline{\bm{X}}
strongly in
H^{4}_{s}
.

Fix a sequence
t_{k}
and steady state
\overline{\bm{X}}
satisfying the above strong convergence. Let
\overline{E}:=E[\overline{\bm{X}}]
. Let
\delta>0
be the size of an
H^{4}_{s}
-neighborhood of
\overline{\bm{X}}
in which (
3.130
) holds. By continuity, for each
k
, there exists a closed interval
I_{k}
containing
t_{k}
such that
\widetilde{\bm{X}}\in B_{\delta}(\overline{\bm{X}})
in the
H^{4}_{s}
topology whenever
t\in I_{k}
. Choose the intervals to be maximal forward-in-time. For the sake of contradiction, suppose that each
I_{k}
is finite, so we may assume they are of the form
[t_{k},T_{k}]
with
T_{k}<+\infty
. By maximality,
\|\widetilde{\bm{X}}(T_{k})-\overline{\bm{X}}\|_{H^{4}_{s}}=\delta
. Then

(3.137)

\frac{d}{dt}(E(t)-\overline{E})^{\beta}=-\beta(E-\overline{E})^{\beta-1}D(t)\overset{\eqref{eq:curvelojarestate}}{\leq}-\beta C^{-1}\sqrt{D(t)}\,.

We integrate the differential inequality (
3.137
) from
t_{k}
to
T_{k}
:

(3.138)

\int_{t_{k}}^{T_{k}}\|\partial_{t}\bm{X}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}\,dt=\int_{t_{k}}^{T_{k}}\sqrt{D(t)}\,dt\lesssim(E(t_{k})-\overline{E})^{\beta}-(E(T_{k})-\overline{E})^{\beta}=o_{k\to+\infty}(1)\,.

By the triangle inequality,

(3.139)

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\bm{X}}(\cdot,T_{k})-\overline{\bm{X}}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\|\bm{X}(\cdot,T_{k})-\bm{X}(\cdot,t_{k})\|_{L^{2}_{s}}}_{=o_{k\to+\infty}(1)\text{ by \eqref{eq:mytimederivest}}}+\|\widetilde{\bm{X}}(\cdot,t_{k})-\overline{\bm{X}}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle=o_{k\to+\infty}(1)\,,

which implies that
\widetilde{\bm{X}}\to\overline{\bm{X}}
in
L^{2}_{s}
as
k\to+\infty
. By the above analysis on sequences, there exists a subsequence such that
\widetilde{\bm{X}}\to\bm{X}
in
H^{4}_{s}
; this contradicts the maximality of
T_{k}
.

We have demonstrated that, for every
0<\delta\ll 1
, there exists
T(\delta)\geq 0
such that
\|\widetilde{\bm{X}}(\cdot,t)-\overline{\bm{X}}\|_{H^{4}_{s}}\leq\delta
whenever
t\geq T
, i.e.,
\widetilde{\bm{X}}\to\overline{\bm{X}}
in
H^{4}_{s}
.

To establish the convergence of
\bm{X}
(compared to
\widetilde{\bm{X}}
), we revisit the inequality (
3.138
), which is now valid on
[T,+\infty)
for
T\gg 1
and demonstrate that
\int_{T}^{+\infty}\|\partial_{t}\bm{X}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}\,dt<+\infty
. In particular,
\bm{X}
is stabilizing in
L^{2}_{s}
and, since
\widetilde{\bm{X}}
is a translation of
\bm{X}
,
\bm{X}
is also stabilizing in
H^{4}_{s}
.
∎

4.
Forcing by intrinsic curvature in 2D: large periodic solutions

In this section, we prove Theorem
1.2
. We consider the planar curve evolution in the free end setting
I=[0,1]
with preferred curvature
\zeta(s,t)
:

(4.1)

\partial_{t}\bm{X}=-\big({\bf I}+\gamma\bm{X}_{s}\otimes\bm{X}_{s}\big)\big(\bm{X}_{sss}-\lambda(s,t)\bm{X}_{s}-(\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{s}\big)_{s}\,,\quad\left\lvert\bm{X}_{s}\right\rvert^{2}=1\,,

where
\bm{e}_{\rm n}(s,t)=\bm{X}_{s}^{\perp}
. With forcing, the relevant boundary conditions become

(4.2)

(\bm{X}_{ss}-\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})\big|_{s=0,1}=0\,,\quad((\bm{X}_{ss}-\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{s}-\lambda\bm{X}_{s})\big|_{s=0,1}=0\,,

equivalent to the curvature conditions

(4.3)

(\kappa-\zeta)\big|_{s=0,1}=0\,,\quad(\kappa-\zeta)_{s}\big|_{s=0,1}=0\,,\quad\lambda=0\,.

The tension equation with forcing is given by

(4.4)

\displaystyle(1+\gamma)\lambda_{ss}-\left\lvert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rvert^{2}\lambda=

\displaystyle-(4+3\gamma)(\bm{X}_{sss}\cdot\bm{X}_{ss})_{s}+\left\lvert\bm{X}_{sss}\right\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle-(1+\gamma)(\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{sss}\cdot\bm{X}_{s}-\gamma(\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{ss}\cdot\bm{X}_{ss}\,.

Due to planarity, the forced system (
4.1
), (
4.2
), and (
4.4
) may be recast as an evolution for the curvature difference
\widetilde{\kappa}=\kappa-\zeta
by the system

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\widetilde{\kappa}

\displaystyle=-\widetilde{\kappa}_{ssss}-\partial_{t}\zeta+\big[(2+\gamma)(\widetilde{\kappa}+\zeta)\lambda_{s}+\lambda(\widetilde{\kappa}+\zeta)_{s}

(4.5)

\displaystyle\qquad+(3+2\gamma)\widetilde{\kappa}_{s}(\widetilde{\kappa}+\zeta)^{2}+(3+\gamma)\widetilde{\kappa}(\widetilde{\kappa}+\zeta)(\widetilde{\kappa}+\zeta)_{s}\big]_{s}

\displaystyle(1+\gamma)\lambda_{ss}-\lambda(\widetilde{\kappa}+\zeta)^{2}

\displaystyle=-(3+2\gamma)\widetilde{\kappa}_{ss}(\widetilde{\kappa}+\zeta)+\widetilde{\kappa}(\widetilde{\kappa}+\zeta)^{3}

(4.6)

\displaystyle\qquad-3(1+\gamma)\widetilde{\kappa}_{s}(\widetilde{\kappa}+\zeta)_{s}-(1+\gamma)\widetilde{\kappa}(\widetilde{\kappa}+\zeta)_{ss}

(4.7)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\kappa}\big|_{s=0,1}

\displaystyle=0\,,\quad\widetilde{\kappa}_{s}\big|_{s=0,1}=0\,,\quad\lambda\big|_{s=0,1}=0\,.

Given a unique solution
(\widetilde{\kappa},\lambda)
to the curvature formulation (
4.5
)-(
4.7
), the curve may be recovered by solving

(4.8)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{X}

\displaystyle=({\bf I}+\gamma\bm{e}_{\rm t}\otimes\bm{e}_{\rm t})((\widetilde{\kappa}\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{s}-\lambda\bm{e}_{\rm t})_{s}\,,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{e}_{\rm t}

\displaystyle=F(\widetilde{\kappa},\zeta,\lambda)\bm{e}_{\rm n}\,,\qquad\partial_{t}\bm{e}_{\rm n}=-F(\widetilde{\kappa},\zeta,\lambda)\bm{e}_{\rm t}\,,

where
\bm{e}_{\rm t}=\bm{X}_{s}
and the expression for
F
is given explicitly in terms of
\widetilde{\kappa},\zeta,\lambda
:

(4.9)

\displaystyle F(\widetilde{\kappa},\zeta,\lambda):=\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\cdot\bm{e}_{\rm n}

\displaystyle=-\widetilde{\kappa}_{sss}+(3+\gamma)\widetilde{\kappa}(\widetilde{\kappa}+\zeta)(\widetilde{\kappa}+\zeta)_{s}+(3+2\gamma)\widetilde{\kappa}_{s}(\widetilde{\kappa}+\zeta)^{2}

\displaystyle+\lambda(\widetilde{\kappa}+\zeta)_{s}+(2+\gamma)(\widetilde{\kappa}+\zeta)\lambda_{s}\,.

The formulation (
4.5
)-(
4.7
) was considered in
[
81
]
,
12
12

12

up to a redefinition of the filament tension
where the authors investigate time-periodic solutions driven by small

(4.10)

\zeta\in C(\mathbb{T}_{T};H^{1}_{s})\,,\quad\partial_{t}\zeta\in C(\mathbb{T}_{T};L^{2}_{s})\,.

Here
\mathbb{T}_{T}:=\mathbb{R}/T\mathbb{Z}
denotes the
T
-periodic torus. Therein, a solution theory was developed in the time-weighted space
\widetilde{\kappa}\in C([0,T];L^{2}_{s})\cap\big\{\|t^{1/4}\|\widetilde{\kappa}(\cdot,t)\|_{H^{1}_{s}}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T)}<+\infty\big\}
.

In this section, we construct time-periodic solutions
without
size restrictions on
\zeta
. It will be convenient to work with solutions in the energy space
\widetilde{\kappa}\in C([0,T];L^{2}_{s})\cap L^{2}_{t}H^{2}_{s}(I\times(0,T))
. The analysis is as in
[
81
]
and Section
3
, except

•

the time-weighted function space in
[
81
]
is replaced by
L^{4}_{t}H^{1}_{s}(I\times(0,T))
in the fixed point argument,

•

we consider short-time theory for arbitrarily large
\zeta
, not only long-time theory for small
\zeta
, and

•

we record the continuous dependence on the initial data.

With these adjustments, the relevant local well-posedness statement is:

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
\zeta
satisfy (
4.10
). Let
\widetilde{\kappa}^{\rm in}\in L^{2}(0,1)
. Then there exists
T^{*}\in(0,+\infty]
and a unique maximal solution

(4.11)

\widetilde{\kappa}\in C([0,S];L^{2}_{s})\cap L^{2}_{t}H^{2}_{s}((0,1)\times(0,S))\,,\quad\forall S<T^{*}\,,

to the curvature formulation (
4.5
)-(
4.7
) with initial data
\widetilde{\kappa}^{\rm in}
.

If
T^{*}<+\infty
, then

(4.12)

\|\widetilde{\kappa}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{s}\cap L^{2}_{t}H^{2}_{s}((0,1)\times(0,S))}\to+\infty\text{ as }S\to T^{*}_{-}\,.

The solution
\widetilde{\kappa}
depends continuously on the initial data
\widetilde{\kappa}^{\rm in}
in the following sense:

Let
\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}
be a solution on
[0,T^{*}_{1})
. Let
S\in(0,T^{*}_{1})
. Then, for all
\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}^{\rm in}
sufficiently close to
\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{\rm in}
, the corresponding solution lives on
[0,S]
, and

(4.13)

\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\to\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}\text{ in }C([0,S];L^{2}_{s})\cap L^{4}_{t}H^{1}_{s}((0,1)\times(0,S))\text{ as }\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}^{\rm in}\to\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{\rm in}\text{ in }L^{2}(0,1)\,.

The proof of Theorem
1.2
will also demonstrate that
T^{*}=+\infty
in Proposition
4.1
.

First, we consider the tension with preferred curvature, analogous to Lemma
3.3
.

Lemma 4.2

(Tension estimate)
.

Let
\bm{X}_{ss}\in H^{1}(0,1)
and
\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n}-\bm{X}_{ss}\in H^{1}_{0}(0,1)
with
|\bm{X}_{s}|^{2}=1
. Then there exists a unique solution
\lambda\in H^{1}_{0}(0,1)
to (
4.4
). The solution satisfies

(4.14)

\|\lambda\|_{H^{1}_{s}}\lesssim\|\bm{X}_{sss}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+(1+\|\bm{X}_{sss}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2})\|\zeta\|_{H^{1}_{s}}\,.

Proof.

The relevant energy identity for the tension is

(4.15)

\displaystyle(1+\gamma)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}|\lambda_{s}|^{2}\,ds+\int_{0}^{1}\left\lvert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rvert^{2}\lambda^{2}\,ds

\displaystyle=-(4+3\gamma)\int_{0}^{1}(\bm{X}_{sss}\cdot\bm{X}_{ss})\lambda_{s}\,ds-\int_{0}^{1}\left\lvert\bm{X}_{sss}\right\rvert^{2}\lambda\,ds

\displaystyle-(1+\gamma)\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{ss}\cdot(\lambda\bm{X}_{s})_{s}\,ds+\gamma\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{ss}\cdot\bm{X}_{ss}\lambda\,ds\,.

We analyze the new terms not present in Lemma
3.3
. Since

(4.16)

\zeta_{ss}\bm{e}_{\rm n}\cdot\big(\lambda_{s}\bm{X}_{s}\big)=0\,,

we rewrite
13
13

13

Technically, this should be justified by writing
\big((\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{ss}\cdot\bm{X}_{s}\big)_{s}=\big(2\zeta_{s}(\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{s}+\zeta(\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{ss}\big)_{s}
at the level of the tension equation.

(4.17)

-\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{ss}\cdot(\lambda\bm{X}_{s})_{s}\,ds=-\int_{0}^{1}(2\zeta_{s}(\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{s}+\zeta(\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{ss})\cdot(\lambda_{s}\bm{X}_{s})\,ds+\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{s}(\lambda\bm{X}_{ss})_{s}\,ds\,,

which, along with the final term of (
4.15
), can be estimated by
(1+\|\bm{X}_{sss}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2})\|\zeta\|_{H^{1}_{s}}\|\lambda\|_{H^{1}_{s}}
. ∎

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

Fix
T>0
and
\zeta
satisfying (
4.10
). We consider the
time-
T
map
(Poincaré map)

(4.18)

\Phi^{T}:L^{2}(0,1)\to L^{2}(0,1)\,,

(4.19)

\Phi^{T}(\widetilde{\kappa}^{\rm in})=\widetilde{\kappa}(\cdot,T)\,,

where
\widetilde{\kappa}
is the solution, granted by Proposition
4.1
, to the curvature formulation with initial data
\widetilde{\kappa}^{\rm in}
.

For
\Phi^{T}
to be well defined, we should demonstrate that every such solution
\widetilde{\kappa}
exists up to time
T
(actually,
T^{*}=+\infty
). Then, moreover,
\Phi^{T}:L^{2}_{s}\to L^{2}_{s}
will be continuous. Let
\widetilde{\kappa}
be a solution. We have the energy identity

(4.20)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{0}^{1}|\bm{X}_{ss}-\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n}|^{2}\,ds

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{1}\big[({\bf I}+\gamma\bm{X}_{s}\otimes\bm{X}_{s})(\bm{Z}-(\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{s})_{s}\big]\cdot\big(\bm{Z}-(\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{s}\big)_{s}\,ds-\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{t}\zeta(\kappa-\zeta)\,ds\,.

Let

(4.21)

\displaystyle E_{\zeta}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\underbrace{|\bm{X}_{ss}-\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n}|^{2}}_{\widetilde{\kappa}^{2}}\,ds

\displaystyle D_{\zeta}

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{1}\big[({\bf I}+\gamma\bm{X}_{s}\otimes\bm{X}_{s})(\bm{Z}-(\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{s})_{s}\big]\cdot\big(\bm{Z}-(\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{s}\big)_{s}\,ds\,.

Then we have the Poincaré inequality

(4.22)

E_{\zeta}\leq c_{0}D_{\zeta}\,,

proved as in (
3.88
), and the differential inequality

(4.23)

\dot{E}_{\zeta}\leq-D_{\zeta}-\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{t}\zeta(\kappa-\zeta)\,ds\leq-\frac{c_{0}}{2}E_{\zeta}+\frac{1}{2c_{0}}\|\partial_{t}\zeta\|_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\,.

We remark that this contractivity is the reason to consider free ends instead of the torus.
By Grönwall’s inequality, we have

(4.24)

E_{\zeta}(t)\leq e^{-\frac{c_{0}t}{2}}E_{\zeta}^{\rm in}+\frac{1}{2c_{0}}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{c_{0}(t-t^{\prime})}{2}}\|\partial_{t}\zeta(\cdot,t^{\prime})\|_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\,dt^{\prime}\,,\quad\forall t\in(0,T^{*})\,,

and additionally

(4.25)

\|D_{\zeta}\|_{L^{1}(0,T^{\prime})}\leq C(M,T^{\prime})

on any finite time interval on which the solution is defined. According to the continuation criterion (
4.12
),
T^{*}=+\infty
.

Next, we demonstrate that
\Phi^{T}
stabilizes a ball. Define

(4.26)

N:=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\partial_{t}\zeta(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\,,\quad N^{\prime}=N+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\zeta(\cdot,t)\|_{H^{1}_{s}}^{2}\,.

From (
4.24
), we have

(4.27)

E_{\zeta}(T)\leq e^{-\frac{c_{0}T}{2}}E_{\zeta}^{\rm in}+\frac{N}{c_{0}^{2}}\,.

Choose
M
satisfying
e^{-\frac{c_{0}T}{2}}M+N/c_{0}^{2}\leq M
,
for example,

(4.28)

M=\frac{N}{(1-e^{-\frac{c_{0}T}{2}})c_{0}^{2}}\,.

Then

(4.29)

\Phi^{T}:\overline{B_{M}^{L^{2}}}\to\overline{B_{M}^{L^{2}}}\,.

Finally, we demonstrate that
\Phi^{T}
as in (
4.29
) is
compact
. This is done via a
smoothing estimate
: We will demonstrate that

(4.30)

\|\widetilde{\kappa}(\cdot,T)\|_{H^{2}_{s}}\leq M^{\prime}(M,T)\,,

that is,

(4.31)

\Phi^{T}:\overline{B_{M}^{L^{2}}}\to\overline{B_{M^{\prime}}^{H^{2}}}\,.

The compactness of the embedding
H^{2}(0,1)\hookrightarrow L^{2}(0,1)
guarantees the compactness of
\Phi^{T}
.

Recall from Section
3.3
that
\sqrt{D_{\zeta}}
controls
\|\bm{X}_{ss}-\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n}\|_{H^{2}_{s}}
, which in turn controls
\|\widetilde{\kappa}\|_{H^{2}_{s}}
. There are two (roughly equivalent) ways to prove the smoothing estimate, both of which go through
D_{\zeta}
. One way is to write

(4.32)

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}(tD_{\zeta}(t))=\frac{1}{2}D_{\zeta}(t)+t\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}D_{\zeta}(t)\,,

substitute an expression for
\frac{d}{dt}D_{\zeta}
, as in Section
3.4
, and close a differential inequality for
tD_{\zeta}(t)
. A second way, which we take, is the following: Let
\widetilde{t}\in[0,T/2]
. By Chebyshev’s inequality, there exists a time
t_{0}\in[0,\widetilde{t}]
such that

(4.33)

D_{\zeta}(t_{0})\leq\widetilde{t}^{-1}C(M,T)\,.

Below, we propagate the quantity
D_{\zeta}(t)
from time
t_{0}
, where it is bounded by (
4.33
), to time
T
. The computations are like those in Section
3.4
.
14
14

14

Technically, one should further justify the computations below, since
a priori
the solution
\widetilde{\kappa}
is not known to belong to
C([t_{0},T];H^{2}(0,1))\cap L^{2}_{t}H^{4}_{s}((0,1)\times(t_{0},T))
. One way would be to prove short-time well-posedness from initial data in
H^{2}(0,1)
, as was done in Section
3
.

Following those computations, we have

(4.34)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}D_{\zeta}(t)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\cdot\partial_{t}(\bm{Z}-(\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{s})\,ds}_{=I_{1}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\gamma\int_{0}^{1}(\bm{X}_{s}\cdot(\bm{Z}-(\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{s})_{s})(\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\cdot(\bm{Z}-(\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{s}))\,ds}_{=I_{2}}\,.

In the term
I_{1}
, we substitute
\bm{Z}=\bm{X}_{sss}-\lambda\bm{X}_{s}
to obtain

(4.35)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\cdot\partial_{t}(\bm{X}_{ss}-(\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n}))_{s}\,ds-\int_{0}^{1}\lambda|\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}|^{2}\,ds

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{1}|\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}|^{2}\,ds+\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\cdot\partial_{t}(\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})\,ds-\int_{0}^{1}\lambda|\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}|^{2}\,ds\,.

We will split various terms by Cauchy’s inequality and absorb
\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}
contributions into the advantageous term on the left-hand side of (
4.35
).

First, we split the term
\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\cdot\partial_{t}(\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{s}\,ds
, leaving a right-hand side contribution of (a constant multiple of)

(4.36)

\|\partial_{t}\zeta\|_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+\|\zeta\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{2}\|\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\,.

Observe the interpolation inequality

(4.37)

\|\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\bm{X}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}+\|\partial_{t}\bm{X}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}^{1/2}\|\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}^{1/2}\,,

keeping in mind that
\|\partial_{t}\bm{X}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}\lesssim D_{\zeta}^{1/2}(t)
. Therefore, (
4.36
) contributes

(4.38)

N^{\prime}+N^{\prime}D_{\zeta}+(N^{\prime})^{2}D_{\zeta}

to the right-hand side remainder. Next,

(4.39)

\left|\int_{0}^{1}\lambda|\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}|^{2}\,ds\right|\leq\|\lambda\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\|\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\,,

which, after applying (
4.37
) and splitting, contributes

(4.40)

\|\lambda\|_{H^{1}_{s}}D_{\zeta}+\|\lambda\|_{H^{1}_{s}}^{2}D_{\zeta}

to the right-hand side. Recalling the tension estimate

(4.41)

\|\lambda\|_{H^{1}_{s}}\lesssim\|\bm{X}_{sss}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+(1+\|\bm{X}_{sss}\|_{L^{2}_{s}})^{2}N^{\prime}\,,

we have

(4.42)

\|\lambda\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{1}_{s}((0,1)\times(0,T))}\lesssim C(M,N^{\prime},T)\,.

Next, we estimate term
I_{2}
by (a constant multiple of)

(4.43)

D_{\zeta}^{1/2}\|\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}\|\bm{Z}-(\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lesssim D_{\zeta}^{1/2}(D_{\zeta}+D_{\zeta}^{1/2}\|\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}^{1/2})\|\bm{Z}-(\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\,.

Recall
\bm{Z}-(\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{s}=\bm{X}_{sss}-\lambda\bm{X}_{s}-(\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{s}
, whose terms we estimate separately as

(4.44)

\displaystyle\|\bm{X}_{sss}-(\zeta\bm{e}_{\rm n})_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim E_{\zeta}^{1/8}D_{\zeta}^{3/8}\,,

\displaystyle\|\lambda\bm{X}_{s}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{1}_{s}((0,1)\times(0,T))}

\displaystyle\leq C(M,N^{\prime},T)\,.

In summary, (
4.43
) contributes

(4.45)

D_{\zeta}^{3/2}(D_{\zeta}^{3/8}E_{\zeta}^{1/8}+\|\lambda\|_{H^{1}_{s}})

to the right-hand side, and, after splitting, we have

(4.46)

[D_{\zeta}(D_{\zeta}^{3/8}E_{\zeta}^{1/8}+\|\lambda\|_{H^{1}_{s}})]^{4/3}\lesssim D_{\zeta}^{11/6}E_{\zeta}^{1/6}+D_{\zeta}^{4/3}\|\lambda\|_{H^{1}_{s}}^{4/3}\,.

Notice that
\big\|D_{\zeta}^{1/3}(t)\|\lambda(\cdot,t)\|_{H^{1}_{s}}^{4/3}\big\|_{L^{1}_{t}(0,T)}\leq C(M,N^{\prime},T)
.

In conclusion, combining (
4.38
), (
4.40
), (
4.45
), and (
4.46
), we have

(4.47)

\frac{d}{dt}D_{\zeta}(t)\leq A(t)D_{\zeta}(t)+B(t)\,,

where
\|A(t)\|_{L^{1}(0,T)},\|B(t)\|_{L^{1}(0,T)}\leq C(M,N^{\prime},T)
. Evolving this differential inequality from time
t_{0}
, we obtain the desired bound on
D_{\zeta}(T)
.

Finally, the Schauder fixed point theorem (Proposition
4.3
) yields the existence of a fixed point and a
T
-periodic solution. ∎

Proposition 4.3

(Schauder fixed point theorem)
.

Let
X
be a Banach space and
K\subset X
be a non-empty bounded, closed, and convex set. Suppose that
\mathcal{A}:K\to K
is compact, in the sense that it is continuous and maps bounded sequences to relatively compact sequences. Then
\mathcal{A}
has a fixed point.

See, e.g.,
[
27
, Corollary 11.2, p. 280]
.

5.
Evolution of immersed rod with material frame in 3D

In this section, we prove Theorem
1.3
. We consider the coupled curve-frame evolution

(5.1)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{X}

\displaystyle=-({\bf I}+\gamma\bm{X}_{s}\otimes\bm{X}_{s})\big(\bm{X}_{sss}-\lambda\bm{X}_{s}-\eta\kappa_{3}\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss}\big)_{s}\,,\quad\left\lvert\bm{X}_{s}\right\rvert^{2}=1\,,

(5.2)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\kappa_{3}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{\eta}{\alpha}+\eta\left\lvert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rvert^{2}\bigg)(\kappa_{3})_{ss}+\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}[\bm{X},\kappa_{3}]\,,

where

(5.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}[\bm{X},\kappa_{3}]

\displaystyle=\eta\big(\left\lvert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rvert^{2}\big)_{s}(\kappa_{3})_{s}+\eta\kappa_{3}\bm{X}_{ssss}\cdot\bm{X}_{ss}+\eta\kappa_{3}\left\lvert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rvert^{4}

\displaystyle\qquad-\bm{X}_{sssss}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss})+\lambda\bm{X}_{sss}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss})

and the tension satisfies

(5.4)

(1+\gamma)\lambda_{ss}-\left\lvert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rvert^{2}\lambda=-(4+3\gamma)(\bm{X}_{sss}\cdot\bm{X}_{ss})_{s}+\left\lvert\bm{X}_{sss}\right\rvert^{2}-\eta\kappa_{3}\bm{X}_{s}\cdot(\bm{X}_{ss}\times\bm{X}_{sss})\,.

The equations are considered on
I=[0,1]
and
\mathbb{T}
. When
I=[0,1]
, we additionally impose the boundary conditions

(5.5)

\bm{X}_{ss}\,,\,\bm{X}_{sss}\big|_{s=0,1}=0\,,\quad\kappa_{3},\lambda\big|_{s=0,1}=0\,.

We again consider (
5.1
) as an evolution for
\bm{X}_{s}
. Note that
\int_{I}\bm{X}\,ds
may be recovered from integrating (
5.1
) in space and time.

Throughout this section, we assume that
\eta\in(0,\eta_{\rm max}]
and
\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{\rm max}]
, and the constants
C
and implied constants in
\lesssim
may depend on
\eta_{\rm max}
,
\alpha_{\rm max}
.

Given the discussion surrounding the curvature criterion (
1.14
), we make the following definition.

Definition 5.1

(Strong solution)
.

Let
T>0
and
I=[0,1]
or
\mathbb{T}
. We say that
(\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3})
is a
strong solution
on
I\times[0,T]
if
|\bm{X}_{s}|^{2}=1
and

(5.6)

(\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3})\in(L^{\infty}_{t}H^{3}_{s}\cap L^{2}_{t}H^{5}_{s})\times(L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{s}\cap L^{2}_{t}H^{2}_{s})\text{ on }I\times(0,T)\,,

(5.7)

{\rm ess}\,\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\|\bm{X}_{ss}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(I)}^{2}<\frac{1}{\alpha}\,,

(5.8)

\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\in L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{s}(I\times(0,T))\,,

and there exists

(5.9)

\lambda\in L^{2}_{t}H^{2}_{s}(I\times(0,T))

such that
(\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3})
satisfy the equations (
5.1
)-(
5.4
) in
\bm{X}_{s}
formulation
15
15

15

in the sense of distributions
with tension
\lambda
. When
I=[0,1]
, we further require the boundary conditions (
5.5
), whereas when
I=\mathbb{T}
, we require that
\bm{X}_{s}
has zero mean.

The energy estimates below can be justified for strong solutions, and moreover
(\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3})\in C([0,T];H^{3}_{s}\times H^{1}_{s})
.
16
16

16

Sometimes we speak of strong solutions on
[0,T]
instead of on
I\times[0,T]
. We say strong solution on
[0,T)
to mean strong solution on
[0,S]
for all
S\in(0,T)
.

The definition of strong solution encodes information coming from the structure of the energy estimates which is not evidently available from a naïve fixed point argument. For example,
\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}
involves
(\kappa_{3})_{sss}
, which is not bounded in
L^{2}_{t,s}
by the regularity requirements (
5.6
). Therefore, even our local well-posedness theory is much more subtle than the curve-only case.

Proposition 5.2

(Local well-posedness)
.

Let

(5.10)

(\bm{X}_{s}^{\rm in},\kappa_{3}^{\rm in})\in H^{3}\times H^{1}(I)\,,\quad|\bm{X}_{s}^{\rm in}|^{2}=1\,,

(5.11)

|\bm{X}_{ss}^{\rm in}|^{2}<\frac{1}{\alpha}\,,

with the relevant boundary conditions. Then there exists a unique maximally defined strong solution
(\bm{X}_{ss},\kappa_{3})
on
[0,T^{*})
with initial data
(\bm{X}_{s}^{\rm in},\kappa_{3}^{\rm in})
.

(Continuation criterion) If
T^{*}<+\infty
, then

(5.12)

\limsup_{T\to T^{*}_{-}}\|\bm{X}_{ss}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}_{s}(I\times(0,T))}^{2}=\frac{1}{\alpha}\,.

Given the previous proposition, we may obtain the following global-in-time consequences:

Proposition 5.3

(Conditional global well-posedness)
.

In the notation of Proposition
5.2
, there exist
C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that if

(5.13)

\|\bm{X}_{ss}^{\rm in}\|_{H^{2}_{s}(I)}+\|\kappa_{3}^{\rm in}\|_{H^{1}_{s}(I)}\leq\frac{1}{C_{1}}\log\frac{1}{C_{2}\alpha}\,,

then
T^{*}=+\infty
.

(Global solutions converge) Suppose that
T^{*}=+\infty
and

(5.14)

\sup_{t\in[0,+\infty)}\|\bm{X}_{ss}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{2}<\frac{1}{\alpha}\,.

Then there exists a steady curve-frame pair
(\overline{\bm{X}},\overline{\bm{e}})\in H^{4}_{s}\times H^{2}_{s}
, where
\overline{\bm{e}}(s)
is a frame vector, such that

(5.15)

(\bm{X},\bm{e})(t)\to(\overline{\bm{X}},\overline{\bm{e}})\text{ in }H^{4}_{s}\times H^{2}_{s}\text{ as }t\to+\infty\,.

Remark 5.4
.

We do not calculate explicit values for
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
(in principle, they can be estimated from the proof), so we do not determine whether the condition (
5.13
) is satisfied in physical parameter regimes. We merely observe that given a value of the slenderness parameter
\epsilon\sim 10^{-2}
–
10^{-3}
, we have
\alpha\sim 10^{-4}
–
10^{-6}
according to (
2.12
).

The key point in both Propositions
5.2
and
5.3
is to prove two
a priori
estimates for strong solutions which propagate the energy
E
and energy dissipation
D
defined in (
5.17
). These are shown in Section
5.1
. In particular, the propagation of
D
estimate is responsible for
\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\in L^{2}_{t,s}
– see Section
5.1.2
. We then proceed directly to the proof of Proposition
5.3
in Section
5.2
, where we exploit the Lojasiewicz inequality from Proposition
6.3
in the periodic setting.

Even with the
a priori
estimates in hand, the existence of strong solutions goes through a hyperviscous regularization (
A.1
)-(
A.2
), in which
\nu\partial_{s}^{4}\kappa_{3}
,
\nu>0
, is incorporated into the
\kappa_{3}
equation. The hyperviscous equations are amenable to fixed point arguments, which we carry out in Appendix
A
. We then obtain a strong solution in the limit as
\nu\to 0
in Section
5.3
. These steps are relegated to the end of the section.

5.1.
Energetics

In analogy to the curve-only setting, we define

(5.16)

\widetilde{\bm{Z}}=\bm{X}_{sss}-\lambda\bm{X}_{s}-\eta\kappa_{3}\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss}

as well as the energy and dissipation quantities

(5.17)

\displaystyle E(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{I}\big(\left\lvert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rvert^{2}+\eta\kappa_{3}^{2}\big)\,ds\,,

\displaystyle D(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{I}\big(({\bf I}+\gamma\bm{X}_{s}\otimes\bm{X}_{s})\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\big)\cdot\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\,ds+\frac{\eta^{2}}{\alpha}\int_{I}(\kappa_{3})_{s}^{2}\,ds\,.

We may write the energy identity (
2.38
) as

(5.18)

\partial_{t}E(t)=-D(t)\,.

This computation can be justified for strong solutions on their domain of definition. Immediately, we have that
E(t)\leq E^{\rm in}
and
\int_{0}^{\infty}D(t)\,dt\leq E^{\rm in}
, as in (
3.94
).

For the free-ended rod we can further show that
E(t)
decays exponentially-in-time and
D(t)
has time-integrated exponential decay, as in (
3.93
). We first note that, by an analogous argument to (
3.86
) in the curve-only setting, we have the following Poincaré-type inequality:

(5.19)

E(t)\leq\frac{1}{2\pi^{4}}D(t)\,.

In addition,
\eta\int_{0}^{1}\left\lvert\kappa_{3}\right\rvert^{2}\,ds\leq\pi^{2}\eta\int_{0}^{1}\left\lvert(\kappa_{3})_{s}\right\rvert^{2}\,ds
by the 1D Poincaré inequality. Using (
5.18
), we thus have

(5.20)

\partial_{t}E(t)\leq-D(t)\leq-c_{0}E(t)\,,

where, as in (
3.89
), we let
c_{0}^{-1}
denote the best constant in (
5.19
). By Grönwall’s inequality, the free-ended rod energy satisfies

(5.21)

E(t)\leq e^{-c_{0}t}E^{\rm in}\,.

Additionally, from (
5.20
) we obtain a time-integrated decay estimate on
D(t)
. For any
\theta\in(0,1)
, we have

(5.22)

\partial_{t}E(t)+\theta D(t)\leq-(1-\theta)D(t)\leq-c_{0}(1-\theta)E(t)\,;

in particular,

(5.23)

\partial_{t}(Ee^{c_{0}(1-\theta)t})+\theta De^{c_{0}(1-\theta)t}\leq 0\,.

Integrating in time and using (
5.21
), we have

(5.24)

\theta\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{c_{0}(1-\theta)t}D(t)\,dt\leq E^{\rm in}

for a global-in-time strong solution.

5.1.1.
Relationships between quantities

We need to establish that the quantities
E(t)
and
D(t)
control
\bm{X}_{ssss}
in a useful way. Recalling the notation
\bm{Z}=\bm{X}_{sss}-\lambda\bm{X}_{s}
from Section
3.3
, we define

(5.25)

\displaystyle D_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{I}\bigg(\left\lvert\bm{Z}_{s}\right\rvert^{2}+\gamma\big(\bm{X}_{s}\cdot\bm{Z}_{s}\big)^{2}+\frac{\eta^{2}}{\alpha}(\kappa_{3})_{s}^{2}\bigg)\,ds\,.

Immediately from (
3.68
) and (
3.69
), we have

(5.26)

\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lesssim D_{1}(t)^{1/2}(1+E(t)^{1/2})+E(t)^{5/2}

in the free end setting and

(5.27)

\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lesssim D_{1}(t)^{1/2}(1+E(t)^{1/2})+E(t)+E(t)^{5/2}

in the closed loop setting.
We need to show that, under the condition
\left\lvert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rvert^{2}\leq\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}
for some
\delta\in(0,1)
(the quantitative variant of (
5.7
)), the full dissipation quantity
D(t)
controls
D_{1}(t)
.

Proposition 5.5
.

Suppose that
(\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3})
satisfies
|\bm{X}_{s}|^{2}=1
and
\|\bm{X}_{ss}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{2}\leq\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}
for some
\delta\in(0,1)
.

In the free end setting, the dissipation
D
and energy
E
bound
D_{1}
and
\bm{X}_{ssss}
as follows:

(5.28)

\displaystyle D_{1}(t)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\delta}D(t)(1+E(t)^{3})+E(t)^{5}\,,

\displaystyle\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\delta}D(t)^{1/2}(1+E(t)^{2})+E(t)^{5/2}+E(t)^{3}\,.

In the closed loop setting,
D
and
E
bound
D_{1}
and
\bm{X}_{ssss}
as:

(5.29)

\displaystyle D_{1}(t)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\delta}D(t)(1+E(t)^{3})+E(t)^{2}+E(t)^{5}\,,

\displaystyle\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\delta}D(t)^{1/2}(1+E(t)^{2})+E(t)+E(t)^{3}\,.

Proof.

We may write

(5.30)

\displaystyle D(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{I}\bigg(\big|\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\big|^{2}+\gamma(\bm{X}_{s}\cdot\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s})^{2}+\frac{\eta^{2}}{\alpha}(\kappa_{3})_{s}^{2}\bigg)\,ds

\displaystyle=\int_{I}\bigg(\big|\bm{Z}_{s}-\eta\big(\kappa_{3}\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss}\big)_{s}\big|^{2}+\gamma(\bm{X}_{s}\cdot\bm{Z}_{s})^{2}+\frac{\eta^{2}}{\alpha}(\kappa_{3})_{s}^{2}\bigg)\,ds

\displaystyle=D_{1}(t)+\eta^{2}\int_{I}\left\lvert\big(\kappa_{3}\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss}\big)_{s}\right\rvert^{2}\,ds-2\eta\int_{I}\bm{Z}_{s}\cdot\big(\kappa_{3}\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss}\big)_{s}\,ds\,,

so that

(5.31)

D_{1}(t)+\eta^{2}\int_{I}\left\lvert\big(\kappa_{3}\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss}\big)_{s}\right\rvert^{2}\,ds=D(t)+2\eta\int_{I}\bm{Z}_{s}\cdot\big(\kappa_{3}\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss}\big)_{s}\,ds\,.

To estimate the right-hand side cross term, we first write

(5.32)

2\eta\left\lvert\bm{Z}_{s}\cdot\big(\kappa_{3}\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss}\big)_{s}\right\rvert\leq 2\eta\left\lvert\bm{Z}_{s}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss})(\kappa_{3})_{s}\right\rvert+2\eta\left\lvert\bm{Z}_{s}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{sss})\kappa_{3}\right\rvert\,.

We rely on the curvature condition (
5.7
) to estimate the first term in (
5.32
) as

(5.33)

2\left\lvert\bm{Z}_{s}\right\rvert\left\lvert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rvert\eta\left\lvert(\kappa_{3})_{s}\right\rvert\leq 2\left\lvert\bm{Z}_{s}\right\rvert(1-\delta)^{1/2}\frac{\eta}{\alpha^{1/2}}\left\lvert(\kappa_{3})_{s}\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon\left\lvert\bm{Z}_{s}\right\rvert^{2}+\frac{1-\delta}{\varepsilon}\frac{\eta^{2}}{\alpha}\left\lvert(\kappa_{3})_{s}\right\rvert^{2}

for
\varepsilon>0
. Taking
\varepsilon=1-\frac{\delta}{2}
yields

(5.34)

2\eta\big|\bm{Z}_{s}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss})(\kappa_{3})_{s}\big|\leq\bigg(1-\frac{\delta}{2}\bigg)\left\lvert\bm{Z}_{s}\right\rvert^{2}+\frac{1-\delta}{1-\frac{\delta}{2}}\frac{\eta^{2}}{\alpha}\left\lvert(\kappa_{3})_{s}\right\rvert^{2}\,;

in particular, both terms may be absorbed into the left-hand side
D_{1}(t)
in (
5.31
).

We split the second term in (
5.32
) as

(5.35)

2\eta\big|\bm{Z}_{s}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{sss})\kappa_{3}\big|\leq\varepsilon\left\lvert\bm{Z}_{s}\right\rvert^{2}+\frac{\eta^{2}}{\varepsilon}\left\lvert\kappa_{3}\right\rvert\left\lvert\bm{X}_{sss}\right\rvert\,,

and for
\varepsilon
sufficiently small (depending on
\delta
), the
\bm{Z}_{s}
term may also be absorbed into the left-hand side
D_{1}(t)
.
To estimate the remainder, we consider the free end and closed loop cases separately.

Free ends
. In the free end setting, using the interpolation inequalities

(5.36)

\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{2}\lesssim\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert(\kappa_{3})_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\,,\quad\lVert\bm{X}_{sss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\lesssim\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\,,

along with the bound (
5.26
), we have

(5.37)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\left\lvert\kappa_{3}\right\rvert^{2}\left\lvert\bm{X}_{sss}\right\rvert^{2}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{2}\lVert\bm{X}_{sss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert(\kappa_{3})_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim E(t)D(t)^{1/2}\big(D_{1}(t)^{1/2}(1+E(t)^{1/2})+E(t)^{5/2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon D_{1}(t)+C(\varepsilon)\big(E(t)^{2}D(t)(1+E(t))+E(t)^{5}\big)\,.

Taking
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small (again depending on
\delta
), we obtain (
5.28
).

Closed loop
.
For the closed filament, the
\bm{X}_{sss}
interpolation inequality (
5.36
) still holds. However, the interpolation inequality for
\kappa_{3}
now requires a full
H^{1}_{s}
norm on the right-hand side. Using (
5.27
), we may then estimate

(5.38)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lvert\kappa_{3}\right\rvert^{2}\left\lvert\bm{X}_{sss}\right\rvert^{2}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{2}\lVert\bm{X}_{sss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}(\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}+\lVert(\kappa_{3})_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}})\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim E(t)^{3/2}\big(D_{1}(t)^{1/2}(1+E(t)^{1/2})+E(t)+E(t)^{5/2}\big)

\displaystyle+E(t)D(t)^{1/2}\big(D_{1}(t)^{1/2}(1+E(t)^{1/2})+E(t)+E(t)^{5/2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon D_{1}(t)+C(\varepsilon)\big(E(t)^{2}D(t)(1+E(t))+E(t)^{2}+E(t)^{5}\big)

for any
\varepsilon>0
. Again taking
\varepsilon
sufficiently small, we obtain (
5.29
).
∎

We must next estimate the tension
\lambda
in terms of
\bm{X}_{ss}
and
\kappa_{3}
.

Lemma 5.6

(Tension estimates)
.

The tension equation (
5.4
) is uniquely solvable in
H^{1}_{0}(I)
, and its solution
\lambda
satisfies

(5.39)

\lVert\lambda\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}+\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{5/4}\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{3/4}\,,

provided that the right-hand side of (
5.39
) is finite.

In addition, given two curve-frame pairs
(\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)},\kappa_{3}^{(1)})
,
(\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)},\kappa_{3}^{(2)})
, let
\lambda^{(1)}
,
\lambda^{(2)}
denote the corresponding tensions. The following difference bound holds:

(5.40)

\displaystyle\lVert\lambda^{(1)}-\lambda^{(2)}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}\big)\times

\displaystyle\times\big(1+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{2}\big)\big(1+\|\kappa_{3}^{(1)}\|_{H^{1}_{s}}+\|\kappa_{3}^{(2)}\|_{H^{1}_{s}}\big)\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}\rVert_{H^{3}_{s}}\|\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}\,,

provided that the right-hand side of (
5.40
) is finite.

Proof.

Estimate (
5.39
) follows from analogous arguments to Lemma
3.3
using the form of (
5.4
).
To show the difference estimate (
5.40
), as in the curve-only setting (see equation (
3.42
)), we let
R_{\lambda}^{(k)}
denote the right-hand side of the tension equation (
5.4
) for curve-frame pair
(\bm{X}_{s}^{(k)},\kappa_{3}^{(k)})
,
k=1,2
. We may estimate the difference

(5.41)

\displaystyle\lVert R_{\lambda}^{(1)}-R_{\lambda}^{(2)}\rVert_{(H^{1})^{*}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}\big(1+(1+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}})\|\kappa_{3}^{(2)}\|_{H^{1}_{s}}\big)\times

\displaystyle\times\big(\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}\big)+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}\rVert_{H^{3}_{s}}\|\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}\,.

Defining
q^{(1)}
,
q^{(2)}
as in (
3.45
), we may obtain the analogous estimate

(5.42)

\displaystyle\lVert q^{(1)}-q^{(2)}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}\big)\times

\displaystyle\times\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{2}(1+\|\kappa_{3}^{(1)}\|_{H^{1}_{s}})\,.

From (
5.41
) and (
5.42
), we obtain (
5.40
) by the same arguments as in (
3.44
).
∎

5.1.2.
Propagation estimates

Equipped with the bounds (
5.28
) and (
5.29
) on
\bm{X}_{ssss}
, we may now turn to estimating
D(t)
.

Proposition 5.7
.

Let
(\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3})
be a strong solution on
[0,T]
satisfying

(5.43)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\bm{X}_{ss}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\leq\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}\,.

Then, in the closed loop setting,

(5.44)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}D(t)

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lvert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rvert^{2}\,ds-\eta\int_{\mathbb{T}}\big(\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss})\big)^{2}\,ds-\frac{\eta^{3}}{2\alpha^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\kappa_{3})_{ss}^{2}\,ds

\displaystyle+C(E,\delta)D(t)^{2}+C(E,\delta)D(t)\,.

In the free end setting,

(5.45)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}D(t)

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\left\lvert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rvert^{2}\,ds-\eta\int_{0}^{1}\big(\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss})\big)^{2}\,ds-\frac{\eta^{3}}{2\alpha^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}(\kappa_{3})_{ss}^{2}\,ds

\displaystyle+e^{-c_{0}t}C(E^{\rm in},\delta)\big(D(t)^{2}+D(t)\big)+C(\delta)(D(t)+D(t)^{2})\,.

Proof.

Differentiating
D(t)
in time, the following identity holds and can be justified for strong solutions:

(5.46)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}D(t)=\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{I}

\displaystyle\bigg(\big|\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\big|^{2}+\gamma(\bm{X}_{s}\cdot\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s})^{2}+\frac{\eta^{2}}{\alpha}(\kappa_{3})_{s}^{2}\bigg)\,ds=J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}\,,

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{I}\big(({\bf I}+\gamma\bm{X}_{s}\otimes\bm{X}_{s})\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\big)\cdot\partial_{t}\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\,ds\,,

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle=\gamma\int_{I}(\bm{X}_{s}\cdot\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s})(\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\cdot\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s})\,ds\,,

\displaystyle J_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta^{2}}{\alpha}\int_{I}(\kappa_{3})_{s}\partial_{t}(\kappa_{3})_{s}\,ds\,.

We have

(5.47)

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle=-\int_{I}\big(({\bf I}+\gamma\bm{X}_{s}\otimes\bm{X}_{s})\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\big)_{s}\cdot\partial_{t}\widetilde{\bm{Z}}\,ds=\int_{I}\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\cdot\partial_{t}\widetilde{\bm{Z}}\,ds

\displaystyle=\int_{I}\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\cdot(\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{sss}-\lambda\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}-\eta\partial_{t}\kappa_{3}\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss}-\eta\kappa_{3}\bm{X}_{s}\times\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss})\,ds\,,

where we have used that
\partial_{t}\widetilde{\bm{Z}}\big|_{s=0,1}=0
in the free end setting and
\bm{X}_{s}\cdot\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}=0
for both free ends and the closed loop. Upon integrating by parts and replacing
\partial_{t}\kappa_{3}
by (
5.2
), we obtain

(5.48)

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle=-\int_{I}\left\lvert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rvert^{2}\,ds-\eta\int_{I}\big(\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss})\big)^{2}\,ds-\int_{I}\lambda\left\lvert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\right\rvert^{2}\,ds

\displaystyle-\frac{\eta^{2}}{\alpha}\int_{I}(\kappa_{3})_{ss}\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss})\,ds-\eta\int_{I}\kappa_{3}\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss})\,ds\,.

The first two terms in (
5.48
) have a favorable sign, but we will need to estimate the remaining three terms. As in the curve-only setting, we estimate

(5.49)

\left\lvert\int_{I}\lambda\left\lvert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\right\rvert^{2}\,ds\right\rvert\leq\lVert\lambda\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\,,

where
\lVert\lambda\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}
may be estimated using the
H^{1}_{s}
bound (
5.39
) of Lemma
5.6
.

Again following the curve-only setting, we now consider the closed loop and free end filaments separately.

Closed loop
.
We begin by noting that, as in (
3.104
), the following interpolation inequality holds for
\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}
:

(5.50)

\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\lesssim\lVert\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\,,

where we have used that
\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}
. Combining (
5.39
) and (
5.50
), we obtain

(5.51)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{\mathbb{T}}\lambda\left\lvert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\right\rvert^{2}\,ds\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon)\,\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\lVert\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C(\varepsilon)\,\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{5/2}\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{3/2}\lVert\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}

for any
\varepsilon>0
. As before, the first term above will be absorbed into the signed first term of
J_{1}
, while the second term above may be estimated as in (
3.106
), using (
5.29
) to obtain

(5.52)

\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\lVert\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\leq C(E,\delta)D(t)\lVert\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+C(E,\delta)\lVert\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\,.

For the third term, again using (
5.29
), we have

(5.53)

\displaystyle\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{5/2}\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{3/2}\lVert\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim E(t)^{9/4}\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{3/2}\lVert\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(E,\delta)D(t)^{7/4}+C(E,\delta)D(t)\,.

We next turn to the fourth term of
J_{1}
. In the periodic setting, we have

(5.54)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\frac{\eta^{2}}{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\kappa_{3})_{ss}\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss})\,ds\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta^{2}}{\alpha}\lVert(\kappa_{3})_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\frac{\eta^{3}}{\alpha^{2}}\lVert(\kappa_{3})_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon)\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{2}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\,,

where the
(\kappa_{3})_{ss}
will later be absorbed into a coercive term
\frac{\eta^{3}}{\alpha^{2}}\lVert(\kappa_{3})_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}
arising from
J_{3}
. The remaining term satisfies

(5.55)

\displaystyle\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{2}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{1/2}\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{3/2}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon)\,\lVert\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{8}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon,\delta)\,D(t)E(t)^{4}

for any
\varepsilon>0
.
We may estimate the final term in
J_{1}
as

(5.56)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\eta\int_{\mathbb{T}}\kappa_{3}\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss})\,ds\right\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{1/4}\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{7/4}

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)\,\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{8}\lVert\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+\varepsilon\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon,\delta)\,E(t)^{4}D(t)+\varepsilon\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}

for any
\varepsilon>0
, where we have used an interpolation inequality for
\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}
and that
\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}
.
In total, for the closed filament, we may obtain the
J_{1}
bound

(5.57)

\left\lvert J_{1}\right\rvert\leq\frac{1}{4}\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+\frac{\eta^{3}}{4\alpha^{2}}\lVert(\kappa_{3})_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+C(E,\delta)D(t)^{2}+C(E,\delta)D(t)\,.

The term
J_{2}
may be estimated exactly as in the curve-only setting. For the periodic filament, we have

(5.58)

\left\lvert J_{2}\right\rvert\lesssim\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lVert\bm{Z}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\leq\varepsilon\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon)\,\lVert\bm{Z}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{4}\leq\varepsilon\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon,\delta)\,D(t)^{2}

for any
\varepsilon>0
.

To estimate
J_{3}
, we observe that, upon differentiating (
5.2
), we have

(5.59)

\partial_{t}(\kappa_{3})_{s}=\big(\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss})\big)_{s}+\frac{\eta}{\alpha}(\kappa_{3})_{sss}\,.

We thus rewrite
J_{3}
as

(5.60)

\displaystyle J_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta^{2}}{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\kappa_{3})_{s}\bigg(\big(\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss})\big)_{s}+\frac{\eta}{\alpha}(\kappa_{3})_{sss}\bigg)\,ds

\displaystyle=-\frac{\eta^{3}}{\alpha^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\kappa_{3})_{ss}^{2}\,ds-\frac{\eta^{2}}{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\kappa_{3})_{ss}\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss})\,ds\,.

The first term is coercive, while the second term has already been estimated in (
5.54
) and (
5.55
). Note from (
5.48
) that these terms appear with the same sign and do not cancel.

Combining the estimates for
J_{1}
,
J_{2}
, and
J_{3}
for the periodic filament, we obtain (
5.44
).

Free ends
.
In the free end setting, the same type of bounds (
5.52
) and (
5.53
) hold for the third term of
J_{1}
, except that we may now build in the exponential decay (
5.21
) of
E(t)
to obtain

(5.61)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{0}^{1}\lambda\left\lvert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\right\rvert^{2}\,ds\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+e^{-c_{0}t}C(E^{\rm in},\varepsilon,\delta)\big(D(t)^{2}+D(t)\big)

\displaystyle+C(\varepsilon,\delta)(D(t)+D(t)^{2})\,.

Here the additional
D(t)+D(t)^{2}
terms come from (
5.28
).

For the fourth term of
J_{1}
, instead of (
5.55
), we need to include lower order terms in the
\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}
interpolation inequality:

(5.62)

\displaystyle\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{2}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{1/2}\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{3/2}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\lVert\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+\lVert\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{1/2}\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{3/2}\big)\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+e^{-c_{0}t}C(E^{\rm in},\varepsilon)\lVert\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}

for any
\varepsilon>0
. Similarly, for the fifth term of
J_{1}
, we must replace (
5.56
) with

(5.63)

\displaystyle\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{1/4}\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{7/4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}(\lVert\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+\lVert\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{1/4}\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{7/4})

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+e^{-c_{0}t}C(E^{\rm in},\varepsilon)\lVert\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}

for any
\varepsilon>0
.
In total, for the free-ended filament, we may bound

(5.64)

\displaystyle\left\lvert J_{1}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+\frac{\eta^{3}}{4\alpha^{2}}\lVert(\kappa_{3})_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle+e^{-c_{0}t}C(E^{\rm in},\delta)\big(D(t)^{2}+D(t)\big)+C(\delta)(D(t)+D(t)^{2})\,.

The term
J_{2}
may again be estimated as in the curve-only setting (
3.113
), (
3.114
), yielding

(5.65)

\left\lvert J_{2}\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon,\delta)(D(t)+D(t)^{2})

for
\varepsilon>0
. The term
J_{3}
is again given by (
5.60
), and the second term in the free end setting may be estimated as in (
5.62
).

Combining the free end estimates for
J_{1}
,
J_{2}
, and
J_{3}
, we have (
5.45
).
∎

5.2.
Conditional GWP and convergence to equilibrium

5.2.1.
Conditional GWP

We now demonstrate Theorem
1.3
that, under certain conditions, the local-in-time solution
(\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3})
to (
1.10
)-(
1.11
) may be continued to a global solution.

We begin by summarizing the consequences of Proposition
5.7
. Namely, by Grönwall’s inequality, we have the
a priori
estimate

(5.66)

D(t)\leq e^{C(E^{\rm in},\delta)}[D^{\rm in}+C(E^{\rm in},\delta)]\,,

valid for a strong solution on a time interval
[0,T]
in both free end and closed loop settings. To prove that certain strong solutions can be continued indefinitely, we should leverage the above estimate to propagate the condition
\|\bm{X}_{ss}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{2}\leq\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}
; we choose
\delta=\frac{1}{2}
.

To propagate this condition, we use the Sobolev embedding

(5.67)

\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}

for both the free ended filament (due to the homogeneous boundary conditions) and the closed loop (since
\bm{X}_{ss}
has zero mean). Recall that
\|\bm{X}_{ssss}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}
is controlled in Proposition
5.5
, which we combine with (
5.66
) and
E(t)\leq E^{\rm in}
to obtain

(5.68)

\|\bm{X}_{ss}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{2}\leq Ce^{C(E^{\rm in})}(D^{\rm in}+1)\,.

The right-hand side is bounded by
\frac{1}{2\alpha}
provided

(5.69)

\|\bm{X}_{ssss}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}\leq\frac{1}{C}\log\frac{1}{C\alpha}\,.

5.2.2.
Convergence to equilibrium

Suppose that
(\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3})
is a global-in-time strong solution satisfying, additionally,

(5.70)

\sup_{t\in[0,+\infty)}\|\bm{X}_{ss}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{2}<\frac{1}{\alpha}\,.

Free end
. Following the arguments in Section
3.4.3
(which rely only on the differential inequalities for
E
and
D
), we can demonstrate in the free end setting that both
E
and
D
converge to zero exponentially as
t\to+\infty
and, from (
5.45
), that
\|\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}
has time-integrated exponential decay. From here, the main point is to demonstrate that the curve
\bm{X}
and frame
\bm{e}
themselves are stabilizing. We demonstrate that
\bm{X}
is stabilizing in
L^{2}_{s}
using (
3.129
). Since

(5.71)

\partial_{s}\bm{e}=(\bm{X}_{ss}\cdot\bm{e})\bm{X}_{s}+\kappa_{3}\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{e}\text{ and }|\bm{e}|^{2}=1\,,

we further obtain that
\partial_{s}\bm{e}
is converging to zero exponentially in
L^{2}_{s}
. Finally, we prove that
\bm{e}
is stabilizing in
L^{2}_{s}
by time-integrating

(5.72)

\partial_{t}\bm{e}=-(\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}\cdot\bm{e})\bm{X}_{s}+\frac{\eta}{\alpha}(\kappa_{3})_{s}\,,

as we did for the curve equation in (
3.129
), and exploiting the time-integrated exponential decay of
\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}
obtained by interpolating between
D^{1/2}
and
\|\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}
. This completes the proof for the free-ended rod.

Closed loop
. We now focus on the closed loop. By the propagation estimates in Proposition
5.7
and arguments in Section
3.4.3
, we have

(5.73)

E(t)\leq E^{\rm in}\,,\quad\lim_{t\to+\infty}D(t)=0\,.

These control
\bm{X}_{ss}
and
\kappa_{3}
but not immediately
\bm{X}
and
\bm{e}
. As in the curve-only setting, we define
\widetilde{\bm{X}}=\bm{X}-\int_{\mathbb{T}}\bm{X}(s,t)\,ds
. By (
5.71
), we have

(5.74)

\limsup_{t\to+\infty}\|\bm{e}\|_{H^{2}_{s}}^{2}<+\infty\,.

First, we demonstrate that
any sequence
(\widetilde{\bm{X}},\bm{e})(s,t_{k})
with
t_{k}\to+\infty
contains a subsequence (not relabeled) converging in
H^{4}_{s}\times H^{2}_{s}
to a steady state.
By weak compactness in
H^{4}_{s}\times H^{2}_{s}
and the Rellich-Kondrachov theorem, there exists a subsequence satisfying

(5.75)

\widetilde{\bm{X}}(s,t_{k})\to\overline{\bm{X}}\text{ in }H^{4-\varepsilon}_{s}\,,\;\forall\varepsilon\in(0,4]\,,\qquad\widetilde{\bm{X}}(s,t_{k})\rightharpoonup\overline{\bm{X}}\text{ in }H^{4}_{s}

(5.76)

\bm{e}(s,t_{k})\to\overline{\bm{e}}\text{ in }H^{2-\varepsilon}_{s}\,,\;\forall\varepsilon\in(0,2]\,,\qquad\bm{e}(s,t_{k})\rightharpoonup\overline{\bm{e}}\text{ in }H^{2}_{s}

(5.77)

\lambda[\widetilde{\bm{X}}(s,t_{k}),\bm{e}(s,t_{k})]\to\lambda[\overline{\bm{X}},\overline{\bm{e}}]\text{ in }H^{1}_{s}\,.

The gradient

(5.78)

\nabla_{g}E=\big((\nabla_{g}E)_{1},(\nabla_{g}E)_{2}\big)=\big((\mathbf{I}+\gamma\bm{X}_{s}\otimes\bm{X}_{s})\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s},-((\nabla_{g}E)_{1,s}\cdot\bm{e})\bm{X}_{s}-\frac{\eta}{\alpha}\partial_{s}\kappa_{3}\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{e}\big)

is weakly continuous in
H^{-1}_{s}
with respect to the above convergences, so
(\overline{\bm{X}},\overline{\bm{e}})
is a steady state:

(5.79)

D[\overline{\bm{X}},\overline{\bm{e}}]=0\,.

The weak convergence of
\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{ssss}
in
L^{2}_{s}
becomes strong convergence once it is known that
\|\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{ssss}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}\to\|\overline{\bm{X}}_{ssss}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}
; this holds, since
D[\bm{X},\bm{e}]\to 0=D[\overline{\bm{X}},\overline{\bm{e}}]
and
(\lambda[\bm{X}_{s}]\bm{X}_{s}+\kappa_{3}\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss})_{s}
converges strongly in
L^{2}_{s}
. Next, since
\partial_{s}\kappa_{3}\to 0
strongly in
L^{2}_{s}
,
\bm{e}
converges strongly in
H^{2}_{s}
, which proves the claim.

The remainder of the analysis is an application of the Lojasiewicz inequality in Proposition
6.3
, as in the curve-only setting, with straightforward modifications.

∎

5.3.
Local well-posedness

Finally, in this section, we prove Proposition
5.2
. As a technical tool, we consider a curve-frame evolution with
hyperviscosity

\nu\partial_{s}^{4}\kappa_{3}
(
\nu>0
) in the frame variable:

(5.80)

\partial_{t}\kappa_{3}+\nu(\kappa_{3})_{ssss}=\bigg(\frac{\eta}{\alpha}+\eta\left\lvert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rvert^{2}\bigg)(\kappa_{3})_{ss}+\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}[\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3}]\,,

where
\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}
is as in (
5.2
), augmented with the boundary conditions

(5.81)

\kappa\,,\,\partial_{s}^{2}\kappa_{3}\big|_{s=0,1}=0\,.

Local well-posedness of the hyperviscous system is shown in Appendix
A
. The computation of
\frac{1}{2}\frac{dD}{dt}
from Section
5.1
can be justified for solutions to the hyperviscous equations, which yields quantitative estimates on
D(t)
pointwise-in-time. Our strong solutions will be obtained in the limit
\nu\to 0^{+}
.

We first prove the following existence statement and establish uniqueness afterward.

Proposition 5.8

(Short-time existence)
.

Let

(5.82)

(\bm{X}_{s}^{\rm in},\kappa_{3}^{\rm in})\in H^{3}\times H^{1}(I)

(5.83)

|\bm{X}_{ss}^{\rm in}|^{2}\leq\frac{1-2\delta}{\alpha}\,.

Then there exists
T>0
, depending only on
\|\bm{X}_{ss}^{\rm in}\|_{H^{2}_{s}}
,
\|\kappa_{3}^{\rm in}\|_{H^{1}_{s}}
, and
\delta
, such that a strong solution exists on
[0,T]
with initial data
(\bm{X}_{s}^{\rm in},\kappa_{3}^{\rm in})
.

Proof.

Let
\nu>0
and
(\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3})
be the short-time solution from Lemma
A.1
.
Recall the energy estimate

(5.84)

E(t)+\int_{0}^{t}D(t^{\prime})\,dt^{\prime}\leq E^{\rm in}\,,

and the propagation estimate from Proposition
5.7
,

(5.85)

D(t)\leq C(\delta,E^{\rm in})(D^{\rm in}+1)\,,

which is valid on any time interval
[0,T]
over which
|\bm{X}_{ss}|^{2}\leq\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}
.

We now use Duhamel’s formula to estimate
\|\bm{X}_{ss}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}
and demonstrate that both the condition
|\bm{X}_{ss}|^{2}\leq\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}
and the estimate (
5.85
) can be propagated up to some time
T
:
17
17

17

A rigorous way to phrase this is as a continuity/bootstrapping argument. Recall that
\|\bm{X}_{ss}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}
varies continuously. We demonstrate that there exists
T
, as in the statement of the lemma, such that the interval on which
\|\bm{X}_{ss}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{2}\leq\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}
is relatively open in
[0,T]
, so it must be the whole
[0,T]
.

(5.86)

\bm{X}_{ss}=e^{-t\partial_{s}^{4}}\bm{X}_{ss}^{\rm in}+R\,,

(5.87)

\displaystyle R(\cdot,t)

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-t^{\prime})\partial_{s}^{4}}\partial_{s}^{2}Q\,dt^{\prime}\,,

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle:=(1+\gamma)\lambda_{s}\bm{X}_{s}+\lambda\bm{X}_{ss}+\eta(\kappa_{3}\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss})_{s}+3\gamma\bm{X}_{s}(\bm{X}_{ss}\cdot\bm{X}_{sss})\,,

where the semigroup is understood with the appropriate boundary conditions.

First, with
N:=\|\bm{X}_{ss}^{\rm in}\|_{H^{2}_{s}}
, we have

(5.88)

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}\|e^{-t\partial_{s}^{4}}\bm{X}_{ss}^{\rm in}\|_{H^{2}_{s}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}e^{-t\partial_{s}^{4}}\bm{X}_{ss}^{\rm in}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{s}(I\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}^{2}\leq CN^{2}\,,

as follows from the two standard energy estimates (multiply the
\bm{X}_{ss}
equation by
\bm{X}_{ss}
and
\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}
, respectively) in Section
3.1
. Hence,

(5.89)

\|e^{-t\partial_{s}^{4}}\bm{X}_{ss}^{\rm in}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{\rm in}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\leq CtN^{2}\,.

In particular, we can interpolate between
L^{2}_{s}
in (
5.89
) and
H^{2}_{s}
in (
5.88
) to obtain

(5.90)

\|e^{-t\partial_{s}^{4}}\bm{X}_{ss}^{\rm in}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{\rm in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{2}\leq Ct^{1/2}N^{2}\,.

Therefore,

(5.91)

\|e^{-t\partial_{s}^{4}}\bm{X}_{ss}^{\rm in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\leq\|\bm{X}_{ss}^{\rm in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}+Ct^{1/4}N\leq\sqrt{\frac{1-2\delta}{\alpha}}+Ct^{1/4}N\,.

Next, we estimate
R
defined in (
5.87
). We have

(5.92)

\displaystyle\|Q\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{s}(I\times(0,T))}

\displaystyle\leq T^{1/2}\|R\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{s}(I\times(0,T))}\leq C(\delta,E^{\rm in},D^{\rm in})T^{1/2}\,,

\displaystyle\|R\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{s}(I\times(0,T))}

\displaystyle\leq C(\delta,E^{\rm in},D^{\rm in})T^{1/2}\,,

\displaystyle\|Q\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{1}_{s}(I\times(0,T))}

\displaystyle\leq C(\delta,E^{\rm in},D^{\rm in})\,,

\displaystyle\|R\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{s}(I\times(0,T))}

\displaystyle\leq C(\delta,E^{\rm in},D^{\rm in})\,,

following the estimate of Lemma
A.2
proved in Appendix
A
.
By interpolating between these estimates, we obtain

(5.93)

\|R(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\leq C(\delta,E^{\rm in},D^{\rm in})T^{1/4}\,.

Combining (
5.91
) and (
5.93
) and taking
T\ll 1
depending only on
\delta
,
E^{\rm in}
, and
D^{\rm in}
, we demonstrate the curvature condition can be propagated to time
T
. Therefore, we have estimates on
E,D\in L^{\infty}_{t}(0,T)
and
\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{ss}\in L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{s}(I\times(0,T))
uniformly in the hyperviscosity coefficient
\nu
.
Finally, we apply standard compactness arguments involving the Aubin-Lions lemma, as in the construction of weak solutions to the Navier-Stokes equations
[
94
, Chapter 4]
,
[
61
, Chapter 12]

[
101
, Chapter 3]
to take
\nu\to 0^{+}
. ∎

With this in hand, we can complete the proof of Proposition
5.2
(local well-posedness) by establishing uniqueness.

Proof of Proposition
5.2
.

Let
(\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)},\kappa_{3}^{(1)})
,
(\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)},\kappa_{3}^{(2)})
be two strong solutions on
[0,T]
with identical initial data, and let
\widetilde{E}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{I}\big(|\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}|^{2}+\eta|\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)}|^{2}\big)\,ds
denote the relative energy. We may calculate the relative energy identity

(5.94)

\displaystyle\frac{d\widetilde{E}}{dt}

\displaystyle+\int_{I}|\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})|^{2}\,ds+\int_{I}\left(\frac{\eta^{2}}{\alpha}+\frac{|\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}|^{2}}{\eta}\right)|\partial_{s}(\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})|^{2}\,ds=\sum_{i=1}^{7}J_{i}\,,

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle=2\eta\int_{I}\big[\partial_{s}\kappa_{3}^{(1)}\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}\times\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\partial_{s}\kappa_{3}^{(2)}\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\times\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}\big]\cdot\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\,ds

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{I}\big[(1+\gamma)(\lambda_{s}^{(1)}\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}-\lambda_{s}^{(2)}\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)})+\lambda^{(1)}\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\lambda^{(2)}\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}\big]\cdot\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\,ds

\displaystyle J_{3}

\displaystyle=2\eta\int_{I}(\kappa_{3}^{(1)}\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}\times\bm{X}_{sss}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)}\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\times\bm{X}_{sss}^{(2)})\cdot\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\,ds

\displaystyle J_{4}

\displaystyle=3\gamma\int_{I}(\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}\cdot\bm{X}_{sss}^{(1)})-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}\cdot\bm{X}_{sss}^{(2)}))\cdot(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\,ds

\displaystyle J_{5}

\displaystyle=\eta^{2}\int_{I}(|\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}|^{2}-|\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}|^{2})(\kappa_{3}^{(2)})_{ss}(\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})\,ds

\displaystyle J_{6}

\displaystyle=\eta^{2}\int_{I}\big[\kappa_{3}^{(1)}(\bm{X}_{ssss}^{(1)}\cdot\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}+|\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}|^{4})

\displaystyle-\kappa_{3}^{(2)}(\bm{X}_{ssss}^{(2)}\cdot\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}+|\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}|^{4})\big](\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})\,ds

\displaystyle J_{7}

\displaystyle=\eta\int_{I}\big[\lambda^{(1)}\bm{X}_{sss}^{(1)}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}\times\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)})-\lambda^{(2)}\bm{X}_{sss}^{(2)}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\times\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\big](\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})\,ds\,.

We will derive the differential inequality

(5.95)

\frac{d\widetilde{E}}{dt}\leq C\widetilde{E}\,,

where the constant
C
may depend on the particulars of the solutions, including the time
T
,
\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}^{(j)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{s}}
,
\lVert(\kappa_{3}^{(j)})_{s}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{s}}
, and
\delta
.

We begin with the only term which requires
|\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}|^{2}<\frac{1}{\alpha}
, which is
J_{1}
. We rewrite
J_{1}
as

(5.96)

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{2\eta\int_{I}\partial_{s}(\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})\big(\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}\times\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}\big)\cdot\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\,ds}_{J_{1a}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{2\eta\int_{I}\partial_{s}\kappa_{3}^{(2)}\big(\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}\times\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\times\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}\big)\cdot\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\,ds}_{J_{1b}}\,.

As long as
|\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}|^{2}\leq\frac{1-2\delta}{\alpha}
, the term
J_{1a}
may be estimated by

(5.97)

\left\lvert J_{1a}\right\rvert\leq(1-\delta)\frac{\eta^{2}}{\alpha}\lVert\partial_{s}(\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+\underbrace{\frac{\alpha}{1-\delta}\sup|\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}|^{2}}_{<1}\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\,,

and therefore both terms may be absorbed into the left-hand side with room to spare.
The remaining term
J_{1b}
may be estimated as

(5.98)

\displaystyle\left\lvert J_{1b}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert\partial_{s}\kappa_{3}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\big(\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}

\displaystyle+\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\big)\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widetilde{E}^{1/2}\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}+\widetilde{E}^{3/8}\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{5/4}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon)\widetilde{E}\,,

for any choice of
\varepsilon>0
, and we note that
C(\varepsilon)
also depends on
\lVert\partial_{s}\kappa_{3}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}
and
\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}
.

We continue term-by-term. To estimate
J_{2}
, we will require the following tension estimate, which is a direct consequence of (
5.40
) from Lemma
5.6
:

(5.99)

\displaystyle\lVert\lambda^{(1)}-\lambda^{(2)}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widetilde{E}^{1/2}+\widetilde{E}^{1/4}\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{1/2}\,.

Using (
5.99
) along with Lemma
5.6
, we may estimate

(5.100)

\displaystyle\left\lvert J_{2}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\lVert\lambda^{(1)}-\lambda^{(2)}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}(1+\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}})

\displaystyle+\lVert\lambda^{(2)}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\big)\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widetilde{E}^{1/2}\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}+\widetilde{E}^{1/4}\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{3/2}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon)\widetilde{E}\,,

again for any choice of
\varepsilon>0
.
We next estimate
J_{3}
as

(5.101)

\displaystyle\left\lvert J_{3}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\lVert\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{sss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}+\lVert\kappa_{3}^{(1)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{sss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle+\lVert\kappa_{3}^{(1)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lVert\partial_{s}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\bigg)\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\widetilde{E}^{1/2}+\widetilde{E}^{1/4}\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{1/2}\big)\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon)\widetilde{E}\,,

and similarly estimate
J_{4}
as

(5.102)

\displaystyle\left\lvert J_{4}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{sss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}+\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{sss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}

\displaystyle+\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lVert\partial_{s}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\bigg)\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widetilde{E}+\widetilde{E}^{3/4}\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon)\widetilde{E}\,.

For
J_{5}
, we integrate by parts once before estimating. We may then obtain the bound

(5.103)

\displaystyle\left\lvert J_{5}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\bigg|\eta^{2}\int_{I}(|\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}|^{2}-|\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}|^{2})(\kappa_{3}^{(2)})_{s}(\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})_{s}\,ds

\displaystyle+2\eta^{2}\int_{I}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}\cdot\bm{X}_{sss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}\cdot\bm{X}_{sss}^{(2)})(\kappa_{3}^{(2)})_{s}(\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})\,ds\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert(\kappa_{3}^{(2)})_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\bigg(\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}+\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lVert\partial_{s}(\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle+\big(\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{sss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}+\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{sss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{sss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\big)\lVert\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\widetilde{E}^{3/8}\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{1/4}\lVert\partial_{s}(\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}+\widetilde{E}^{3/4}\lVert\partial_{s}(\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\widetilde{E}^{1/2}\lVert\partial_{s}(\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{1/2}\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)\widetilde{E}+\varepsilon\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+\varepsilon\lVert\partial_{s}(\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\,.

Here
C(\varepsilon)
again depends on the particulars of the solutions. We may next estimate
J_{6}
as

(5.104)

\displaystyle\left\lvert J_{6}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\big(\lVert\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}+\lVert\kappa_{3}^{(1)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\big)\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}

\displaystyle+\lVert\kappa_{3}^{(1)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}^{(1)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}+\lVert\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{4}

\displaystyle+\lVert\kappa_{3}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\big(\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{3}+\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{3}\big)\bigg)\lVert\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widetilde{E}^{3/4}\lVert\partial_{s}(\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{1/2}+\widetilde{E}^{1/2}\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle+\widetilde{E}^{5/8}\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{1/4}+\widetilde{E}

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)\widetilde{E}+\varepsilon\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+\varepsilon\lVert\partial_{s}(\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\,.

Finally, using (
5.99
) and Lemma
5.6
, we may estimate
J_{7}
as

(5.105)

\displaystyle\left\lvert J_{7}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\lVert\lambda^{(1)}-\lambda^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{sss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}

\displaystyle+\lVert\lambda^{(1)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\big(\lVert\partial_{s}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}+\lVert\bm{X}_{sss}^{(1)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle+\lVert\bm{X}_{sss}^{(1)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\big)\bigg)\lVert\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widetilde{E}+\widetilde{E}^{3/4}\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon)\widetilde{E}\,.

Altogether, taking
\varepsilon
sufficiently small, we may absorb the right-hand side terms
\lVert\partial_{s}^{2}(\bm{X}_{ss}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{ss}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}
and
\lVert\partial_{s}(\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}
into the left-hand side of (
5.94
) to obtain the relative energy inequality (
5.95
).
∎

6.
Lojasiewicz inequalities

To demonstrate the convergence to equilibrium for (
1.1
) and (
1.10
)-(
1.11
) on the periodic interval
I=\mathbb{T}
, we require a powerful tool from the theory of gradient flows known as the
Lojasiewicz inequality
. Even in finite dimensions, it is possible for solutions to the gradient flow
\dot{x}=-\nabla V
of a smooth potential
V
to asymptotically wind in such a way that different subsequences converge to different equilibria; see the “goat tracks” in
[
99
, p. 69]
. However, this is impossible for analytic
V
due to the Lojasiewicz inequality, which may be regarded as a piece of algebraic geometry. In
[
98
]
, Simon discovered how to prove and exploit the Lojasiewicz inequality for certain analytic energies by reducing the inequality to finite dimensions using ellipticity. There is now an abstract framework in which to prove many such inequalities. We use this framework to prove a Lojasiewicz inequality first in the curve-only setting and then in the coupled curve-frame setting.

6.1.
Curves

First, we consider the manifold structure of the space of curves. One may view this as a framework in which to make the gradient calculation in Section
2
rigorous. Let

(6.1)

\mathbf{M}:=\big\{\bm{X}\in H^{4}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R}^{3})\,:\,|\bm{X}_{s}|^{2}=1\;\forall s\in\mathbb{T}\big\}\,.

We may locally coordinatize
\mathbf{M}
in the following way. Let
\bm{X}\in\mathbf{M}
. Define the tangent space

(6.2)

T_{\bm{X}}\mathbf{M}:=\big\{\bm{Y}\in H^{4}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R}^{3})\,:\,\bm{Y}_{s}\cdot\bm{X}_{s}=0\;\forall s\in\mathbb{T}\big\}\,,

endowed with the
H^{4}
topology. In a neighborhood
U
of the origin in
T_{\bm{X}}\mathbf{M}
, we have the exponential map

(6.3)

{\rm Exp}_{\bm{X}}:U\subset T_{\bm{X}}\mathbf{M}\to\mathbf{M}\,,

defined pointwise through the exponential map on the sphere:

(6.4)

({\rm Exp}_{\bm{X}}\bm{Y})_{s}(s)=\exp_{\bm{X}_{s}(s)}^{S^{2}}\bm{Y}_{s}(s)\,,\quad({\rm Exp}_{\bm{X}}\bm{Y})(0)=\bm{X}(0)+\bm{Y}(0)\,.

Then
{\rm Exp}_{\bm{X}}
is analytic into the ambient space (since
S^{2}
is analytic and the map is defined pointwise) and provides a local coordinate chart around
\bm{X}
.
18
18

18

We need local coordinates to apply Proposition
6.2
. The particulars of the exponential map will not enter the computations except for property
d|_{0}\rm{Exp}_{(\overline{\bm{X}},\overline{\bm{e}})}={\rm Id}
.
The above charts can be used to give
\mathbf{M}
the structure of an analytic Hilbert manifold which is embedded in the ambient space
H^{4}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R}^{3})
. However, all of our calculations will take place in a single chart.

For
\gamma\geq 0
, we endow
\mathbf{M}
with the metric

(6.5)

g^{\gamma}_{\bm{X}}(\bm{Y},\bm{Z})=\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{I}+\gamma\bm{X}_{s}\otimes\bm{X}_{s})^{-1}\bm{Y}\cdot\bm{Z}\,ds\,,\quad\forall\bm{Y},\bm{Z}\in T_{\bm{X}}\mathbf{M}

and define the energy functional
E:\mathbf{M}\to\mathbb{R}
by

(6.6)

E=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}}|\bm{X}_{ss}|^{2}\,ds\,,

both of which are analytic on the ambient space and hence also analytic when restricted to
\mathbf{M}
.

Proposition 6.1

(Curve Lojasiewicz inequality)
.

Let
\bm{X}\in\mathbf{M}
be a critical point for the energy:
d|_{\bm{X}}E=0
. Then there exists
\delta(\bm{X})>0
and
\beta\in(0,1/2)
such that if
\bm{Y}\in\mathbf{M}
with
\|\bm{X}-\bm{Y}\|_{H^{4}}\leq\delta
and
\gamma\geq 0
, then

(6.7)

|E(\bm{X})-E(\bm{Y})|^{1-\beta}\lesssim_{\gamma}\sqrt{D_{\gamma}[\bm{Y}]}\,,

where, as in Section
3.3
,

(6.8)

D_{\gamma}[\bm{Y}]=\int_{\mathbb{T}}((\mathbf{I}+\gamma\bm{X}_{s}\otimes\bm{X}_{s})\bm{Z}_{s})\cdot\bm{Z}_{s}\,ds\,,\quad\bm{Z}=\bm{Y}_{sss}-\lambda[\bm{Y}]\bm{Y}_{s}\,,

with
\lambda[\bm{Y}]
as in (
6.21
).

One may compare with the statement of the
\gamma=0
planar case in
[
52
, Proposition 8.5, p. 434]
.
