We rely on an abstract Lojasiewicz inequality which generalizes the approach in
[
98
,
99
]
, see, e.g.
[
41
,
20
]
. The following variant is adapted from
[
51
, Theorem 2.4.2]
.

Proposition 6.2
.

Suppose we are given the following data:

(1)

A Hilbert space
(H,\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{H})
and a Banach space
X
densely embedded into
H
.

(2)

An analytic functional
E:U\to\mathbb{R}
which is defined on an open neighborhood
U\subset X
of the origin.

(3)

An analytic metric
g:U\to{\rm Sym}_{2}(U;\mathbb{R})
with
g\approx\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{H}
as bilinear forms in
U
:

(6.9)

\frac{1}{C}g_{u}(v,v)\leq\langle v,v\rangle_{H}\leq Cg_{u}(v,v)\,,\quad\forall u\in U\,,\;v\in H\,.

(4)

There is an analytic map
\nabla_{g}E:U\to H
so that for every
u\in U
and
x\in X

(6.10)

d|_{u}E(x):=\frac{d}{dt}E(u+tx)|_{t=0}=\langle\nabla_{g}E(u),x\rangle_{g}\,.

(5)

Suppose that
\nabla_{g}E(0)=0
and
\mathcal{L}:=d|_{0}\nabla_{g}E
is a Fredholm operator of index zero from
X
to
H
.

Then there is
\beta\in(0,1/2)
,
A<\infty
, and a neighborhood
\mathcal{O}\subset U
of the origin so that

(6.11)

|E(u)-E(0)|^{1-\beta}\leq A\|\nabla_{g}E(u)\|_{g}\,,\quad\forall u\in\mathcal{O}\,.

The statement in
[
51
]
is written for the special case when the metric
g=\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{H}
(that is, independent of
u
). We can obtain Proposition
6.2
from this special case in the following way:

Proof.

According to
[
51
]
, Proposition
6.2
holds assuming that

(6.12)

\nabla_{H}E:U\to H\text{ is analytic, and }d|_{0}\nabla_{H}E:X\to H\text{ is Fredholm index zero}\,,

and the conclusion is that

(6.13)

|E(u)-E(0)|^{1-\beta}\leq A\|\nabla_{H}E(u)\|_{H}\,.

Our goal is two-fold: Prove (
6.12
) from our assumptions on
\nabla_{g}E
, and obtain the conclusion (
6.11
) from (
6.13
).

Suppose that the hypotheses are satisfied with the analytic metric
g
. The map
g|_{u}^{\flat}:H\to H^{*}
,
g|_{u}^{\flat}(v)=g(v,\cdot)
, and its inverse
g|_{u}^{\sharp}
from Riesz representation are analytic. We also define
h^{\sharp}:H^{*}\to H
to be the Riesz representation map corresponding to
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{H}
. Therefore, under the assumptions,

(6.14)

u\mapsto d|_{u}E=g|_{u}^{\flat}\nabla_{g}E(u)\in H^{*}

is analytic, so
u\mapsto\nabla_{H}E(u)=h^{\sharp}d|_{u}E
is analytic.

Next, we verify the Fredholm property.

(6.15)

d|_{u}\nabla_{H}E=d|_{u}h^{\sharp}dE=d|_{u}h^{\sharp}g|_{u}^{\flat}(\nabla_{g}E)=h^{\sharp}(d|_{u}g^{\flat})\nabla_{g}E+h^{\sharp}g|_{u}^{\flat}d|_{u}\nabla_{g}E\,,

and we evaluate at the origin, which is a critical point, thereby obtaining

(6.16)

d|_{u}\nabla_{H}E=h^{\sharp}g|_{0}^{\flat}d|_{0}\nabla_{g}E\,.

The right-hand side is a post-composition of the Fredholm index zero operator with an isomorphism of the Hilbert space
H
. Therefore,
d|_{0}\nabla_{H}E
is Fredholm index zero, and we have verified (
6.12
).

The right-hand side of the conclusion (
6.13
) is

(6.17)

\|\nabla_{H}E(u)\|_{H}=\sup_{\|v\|_{H}\leq 1}\langle\nabla E(u),v\rangle_{H}=\sup_{\|v\|_{H}\leq 1}d|_{u}E(v)\,.

We have the analogous characterization

(6.18)

\|\nabla_{g}E(u)\|_{g}=\sup_{\|v\|_{g}\leq 1}\langle\nabla_{g}E(u),v\rangle_{g}=\sup_{\|v\|_{g}\leq 1}d|_{u}E(v)\,.

Since
\|\cdot\|_{H}
and
\|\cdot\|_{g}
are equivalent in a neighborhood of
u=0
, the desired conclusion (
6.11
) follows from (
6.13
) and the above characterizations of the norms.
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.1
.

We now verify the hypotheses of Proposition
6.3
. We begin with the case
\gamma=0
; we recover
\gamma\geq 0
at the end as a corollary. Fix
\overline{\bm{X}}\in\mathbf{M}
. We are applying the proposition in coordinates at
\overline{\bm{X}}
, namely, in a neighborhood
U
of the origin in
T_{\overline{\bm{X}}}\mathbf{M}
. (Although the metric (
6.5
)
looks
independent of
\bm{X}
, it is variable in the coordinate patch.) We work in the Banach space
X=T_{\overline{\bm{X}}}\mathbf{M}
, and the Hilbert space

(6.19)

H=\big\{\bm{Y}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R}^{3})\,:\,\bm{Y}_{s}\cdot\bm{X}_{s}=0\text{ in }H^{-1}\big\}\,.

At the origin in
T_{\overline{\bm{X}}}\mathbf{M}
,
g=g^{0}
coincides with the
L^{2}
inner product on the tangent space. By analyticity, it is therefore bounded above and below by the
L^{2}
inner product on a neighborhood of the origin. The gradient computation in Section
2
, specifically, (
2.23
)-(
2.25
), yields
19
19

19

Moreover, when there is no preferred curvature, the calculation can be greatly simplified.

(6.20)

\nabla_{g}E=(\bm{X}_{sss}-\lambda[\bm{X}]\bm{X}_{s})_{s}\,,

which is analytic. The Lagrange multiplier
\lambda[\bm{X}]:H^{4}\to H^{2}
is the solution to

(6.21)

\lambda_{ss}-|\bm{X}_{ss}|^{2}\lambda+4(\bm{X}_{sss}\cdot\bm{X}_{ss})_{s}-|\bm{X}_{sss}|^{2}=0\,,

whose analyticity can be demonstrated via the implicit function theorem.

Let
\widetilde{E}=E\circ{\rm Exp}_{\overline{\bm{X}}}
and
\widetilde{g}
be the metric in coordinates. Then

(6.22)

\nabla_{g}E\circ{\rm Exp}_{\overline{\bm{X}}}=d{\rm Exp}_{\overline{\bm{X}}}\nabla_{\widetilde{g}}\widetilde{E}\,,\quad\text{ in }U\,.

We calculate
d|_{0}
of the above expression:

(6.23)

d|_{\overline{\bm{X}}}\nabla_{g}E\underbrace{d|_{0}{\rm Exp}}_{={\rm Id}}=d|_{0}d{\rm Exp}_{\overline{\bm{X}}}\underbrace{(\nabla_{\widetilde{g}}\widetilde{E})(0)}_{=0}+\underbrace{d|_{0}{\rm Exp}_{\overline{\bm{X}}}}_{={\rm Id}}d|_{0}\nabla_{\widetilde{g}}\widetilde{E}\,,

that is,

(6.24)

d|_{\overline{\bm{X}}}\nabla_{g}E=d|_{0}\nabla_{\widetilde{g}}\widetilde{E}:T_{\overline{\bm{X}}}\mathbf{M}\to H\,,

so we can study the linearized operator without reference to coordinates.
We compute

(6.25)

\mathcal{L}[\bm{Y}]=d|_{\overline{\bm{X}}}\nabla_{g}E[\bm{Y}]=\big(\bm{Y}_{sss}-(d|_{\overline{\bm{X}}}\lambda)[\bm{Y}]\bm{X}_{0,s}-\lambda[\overline{\bm{X}}]\bm{Y}_{s}\big)_{s}\,.

To demonstrate the Fredholm property, we choose a smooth frame
\bm{e}_{1}(s),\bm{e}_{2}(s)
on
\overline{\bm{X}}
, perpendicular to
\bm{X}_{0,s}
, and make the identification
T_{\overline{\bm{X}}}\mathbf{M}\cong H^{3}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R})^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
under the map

(6.26)

\bm{Y}\mapsto(c_{1},c_{2},\vec{c}_{0}):=\left(\bm{Y}_{s}\cdot\bm{e}_{1},\bm{Y}_{s}\cdot\bm{e}_{2},\int_{\mathbb{T}}\bm{Y}\,ds\right)\,.

Likewise, we identify the Hilbert space
H
in (
5.7
) with
H^{-1}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R})^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
. To calculate
\mathcal{L}
under this identification, we write
\bm{Y}_{s}=c_{1}\bm{e}_{1}+c_{2}\bm{e}_{2}
and compute
\mathcal{L}_{s}\cdot\bm{e}_{1}
and
\mathcal{L}_{s}\cdot\bm{e}_{2}
. (The action of the equation on the center of mass
\vec{c}_{0}
is trivial.) Since

(6.27)

\bm{Y}_{sssss}=c_{1,ssss}\bm{e}_{1}+c_{2,ssss}\bm{e}_{2}+\cdots\,,

where the remainder belongs to
L^{2}
, the linearized operator
\mathcal{L}
in (
6.25
) is realized as

(6.28)

\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}=(c_{1},c_{2},\vec{c}_{0})\mapsto\begin{bmatrix}\partial_{s}^{4}+\widetilde{A}&B&\\
C&\partial_{s}^{4}+\widetilde{D}&\\
&&0\end{bmatrix}:H^{3}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R})^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\to H^{-1}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{R})^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\,,

where
\widetilde{A},B,C,\widetilde{D}
map into
L^{2}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R})
. Let

(6.29)

P=\begin{bmatrix}(1+\partial_{s}^{4})^{-1}&&\\
&(1+\partial_{s}^{4})^{-1}&\\
&&1\end{bmatrix}\,.

Then

(6.30)

P\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}={\rm Id}+P\begin{bmatrix}-1+\widetilde{A}&B&\\
C&-1+\widetilde{D}&\\
&&-1\end{bmatrix}:H^{3}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R})^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\circlearrowleft

is a compact perturbation of the identity and hence Fredholm index zero.

Finally, to incorporate
\gamma\geq 0
, we observe that
g^{\gamma}
and
g=g^{0}
induce locally equivalent norms, so the conclusions of the Lojasiewicz inequality are equivalent, according to (
6.18
). ∎

6.2.
Framed curves

We now introduce the manifold structure on the space of framed curves

(6.31)

\mathbf{M}_{\rm f}:=\big\{(\bm{X},\bm{e})\in\mathbf{M}\times H^{2}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R}^{3})\,:\,|\bm{e}|^{2}=1\,,\;\bm{e}\cdot\bm{X}_{s}=0\,,\;\forall s\in\mathbb{T}\big\}\,.

The tangent space, realized in the ambient space, is

(6.32)

T_{(\bm{X},\bm{e})}\mathbf{M}_{\rm f}=\big\{(\bm{Y},\bm{b})\in T_{\bm{X}}\mathbf{M}\times H^{2}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R}^{3})\,:\,\bm{b}\cdot\bm{e}=0\,,\;\bm{b}\cdot\bm{X}_{s}+\bm{e}\cdot\bm{Y}_{s}=0\,,\;\forall s\in\mathbb{T}\big\}\,.

It will sometimes be natural to parameterize

(6.33)

\bm{b}=(\bm{e}\cdot\bm{Y}_{s})\bm{X}_{s}+\underbrace{\theta\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{e}}_{\widetilde{\bm{b}}}\,,

which reflects that the frame moves passively with the curve, and
\theta
is the independent degree of freedom associated with the frame. More concretely, we have the identification

(6.34)

\iota_{\bm{X},\bm{e}}:T_{\bm{X}}\mathbf{M}\times H^{2}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R})\to T_{(\bm{X},\bm{e})}\mathbf{M}_{\rm f}

(6.35)

\iota_{\bm{X},\bm{e}}(\bm{Y},\theta)=(\bm{Y},(\bm{e}\cdot\bm{Y}_{s})\bm{X}_{s}+\theta\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{e})\,.

Next, the exponential map
\exp_{\bm{X}_{s}(s)}\bm{Y}_{s}(s)
induces a rotation
Q[\bm{X}_{s},\bm{Y}_{s}](s)\in SO(3)
. Then

(6.36)

{\rm Exp}_{(\bm{X},\bm{e})}(\bm{Y},\bm{b})=({\rm Exp}_{\bm{X}}\bm{Y},Q[\bm{X}_{s},\bm{Y}_{s}](s)\exp_{\bm{e}}^{S_{2}}\widetilde{\bm{b}})

is analytic. For
\gamma\geq 0
and
\alpha>0
, we endow
\mathbf{M}_{\rm f}
with the metric

(6.37)

g^{\gamma,\alpha}_{\bm{X},\bm{e}}(\bm{Y},\bm{b};\bm{Z},\bm{c})=\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{I}+\gamma\bm{X}_{s}\otimes\bm{X}_{s})^{-1}\bm{Y}\cdot\bm{Z}\,ds+\alpha\int_{\mathbb{T}}\theta(s)\phi(s)\,ds\,,

where
\theta,\phi
are as in (
6.33
). For
\eta>0
, we define the energy functional

(6.38)

E_{\eta}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}}|\bm{X}_{ss}|^{2}\,ds+\frac{\eta}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}}|\kappa_{3}|^{2}\,ds\,,

where

(6.39)

\kappa_{3}[\bm{X},\bm{e}]=\partial_{s}\bm{e}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{e}):H^{4}\times H^{2}\to H^{1}\,.

Proposition 6.3

(Rod Lojasiewicz inequality)
.

Let
(\bm{X},\bm{e})\in\mathbf{M}_{\rm f}
. There exists
\delta(\bm{X},\bm{e})>0
and
\beta\in(0,1/2)
such that if
(\bm{Y},\mathbf{b})\in\mathbf{M}_{\rm f}
with
\|(\bm{X},\bm{e})-(\bm{Y},\mathbf{b})\|_{H^{4}\times H^{2}}\leq\delta
and
\eta>0
,
\gamma\geq 0
, and
\alpha>0
, then

(6.40)

|E_{\eta}(\bm{X},\bm{e})-E_{\eta}(\bm{Y},\mathbf{b})|^{1-\beta}\lesssim_{\eta,\gamma,\alpha}\sqrt{D_{\eta,\gamma,\alpha}[\bm{Y},\bm{b}]}\,,

where, as in Section
5.1
,

(6.41)

\displaystyle D_{\eta,\gamma,\alpha}[\bm{Y},\bm{b}]

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}}((\mathbf{I}+\gamma\bm{X}_{s}\otimes\bm{X}_{s})\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s})\cdot\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\,ds\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{Z}}

\displaystyle=\bm{Y}_{sss}-\lambda[\bm{Y},\bm{b}]\bm{Y}_{s}-\eta\kappa_{3}[\bm{Y},\bm{b}]\bm{Y}_{s}\times\bm{Y}_{ss}\,,

with
\lambda[\bm{Y},\bm{b}]
as in (
6.45
).

Proof of Proposition
6.3
.

We proceed with calculations analogous to the curve case. Define the Banach space
X=T_{(\overline{\bm{X}},\overline{\bm{e}})}\mathbf{M}_{\rm f}
and Hilbert space

(6.42)

H=\big\{(\bm{Y},\bm{b})\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}\,:\,\bm{Y}_{s}\cdot\bm{X}_{s}=0\,,\;\bm{b}\cdot\bm{e}=0\,,\;\bm{b}\cdot\bm{X}_{s}+\bm{e}\cdot\bm{Y}_{s}=0\,\text{ in }H^{-1}(\mathbb{T})\big\}\,.

We begin with
\gamma=0
and we suppress the dependence of
g
on
\alpha
. The gradient
\nabla_{g}E
with
g
as in (
6.37
) will have two components. From the calculations in Section
2
, specifically, (
2.23
)-(
2.27
), we have

(6.43)

(\nabla_{g}E)_{1}=(\bm{X}_{sss}-\lambda[\bm{X},\bm{e}]\bm{X}_{s}-\eta\kappa_{3}\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss})_{s}

(6.44)

(\nabla_{g}E)_{2}=-((\nabla_{g}E)_{1,s}\cdot\bm{e})\bm{X}_{s}-\frac{\eta}{\alpha}(\kappa_{3})_{s}\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{e}\,,

where
\lambda[\bm{X},\bm{e}]:H^{4}\times H^{2}\to H^{2}
is the solution
\lambda
to

(6.45)

\lambda_{ss}-|\bm{X}_{ss}|^{2}\lambda=-4(\bm{X}_{sss}\cdot\bm{X}_{ss})_{s}+|\bm{X}_{sss}|^{2}-\eta\kappa_{3}\bm{X}_{s}\cdot(\bm{X}_{ss}\times\bm{X}_{sss})\,.

Let
(\overline{\bm{X}},\overline{\bm{e}})
be a rod equilibrium, which is smooth with twist
\kappa_{3}\equiv C_{0}
constant. Then

(6.46)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{1}[\bm{Y},\bm{b}]

\displaystyle=d|_{(\overline{\bm{X}},\overline{\bm{e}})}\nabla_{g}E[\bm{Y},\bm{b}]_{1}

\displaystyle=\big(\bm{Y}_{sss}-d|_{(\overline{\bm{X}},\overline{\bm{e}})}\lambda[\bm{Y},\bm{b}]\bm{X}_{s}-\lambda[\overline{\bm{X}},\overline{\bm{e}}]\bm{Y}_{s}\big)_{s}

\displaystyle-\eta\big(d|_{(\overline{\bm{X}},\overline{\bm{e}})}\kappa_{3}[\bm{Y},\bm{b}]\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss}+C_{0}(\bm{Y}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss}+\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{Y}_{ss})\big)_{s}

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{2}[\bm{Y},\bm{b}]

\displaystyle=d|_{(\overline{\bm{X}},\overline{\bm{e}})}\nabla_{g}E[\bm{Y},\bm{b}]_{2}

\displaystyle=-(\mathcal{L}_{1}[\bm{Y},\bm{b}]_{s}\cdot\bm{e})\bm{X}_{s}-\frac{\eta}{\alpha}\big(d|_{(\overline{\bm{X}},\overline{\bm{e}})}\kappa_{3}[\bm{Y},\bm{b}]\big)_{s}\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{e}\,,

where in the
\mathcal{L}_{2}
calculation we have used that
(\overline{\bm{X}},\overline{\bm{e}})
is a critical point. We compute

(6.47)

d|_{(\bm{X},\bm{e})}\kappa_{3}[\bm{Y},\bm{b}]=\partial_{s}\bm{b}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{e})+\partial_{s}\bm{e}\cdot(\bm{Y}_{s}\times\bm{e}+\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{b})\,.

It will be advantageous to rewrite
\mathcal{L}=d|_{(\overline{\bm{X}},\overline{\bm{e}})}\nabla_{g}E
under the map
\iota_{\bm{X},\bm{e}}
and examine the
\theta
component of
\mathcal{L}
. This is done by writing
\bm{b}
as in (
6.33
) and computing
\mathcal{L}_{2}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{e})
:

(6.48)

-\frac{\eta}{\alpha}\big(d|_{(\overline{\bm{X}},\overline{\bm{e}})}\kappa_{3}[\bm{Y},\bm{b}]\big)_{s}=-\frac{\eta}{\alpha}\theta_{ss}+R\,,

where
R
maps into
H^{1}
.
As in the curve-only setting (
6.26
), we identify
\bm{Y}
with its coefficients
c_{1},c_{2}
in a smooth frame and a center of mass
\vec{c}_{0}
. Under these identifications, the linearized operator
\mathcal{L}
becomes, compared to (
6.28
), the block operator

(6.49)

\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}:(c_{1},c_{2},\vec{c}_{0},\theta)\mapsto\begin{bmatrix}\partial_{s}^{3}+\widetilde{A}_{11}&A_{12}&&-\eta(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss})\cdot\bm{e}_{1}\partial_{ss}+\widetilde{A}_{14}\\
A_{21}&\partial_{s}^{3}+\widetilde{A}_{22}&&-\eta(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss})\cdot\bm{e}_{2}\partial_{ss}+\widetilde{A}_{24}\\
&&0&\\
B_{41}&B_{42}&&-\frac{\eta}{\alpha}\partial_{ss}+\widetilde{B}_{44}\end{bmatrix}

(6.50)

H^{3}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R})^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times H^{2}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R})\to H^{-1}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{R})^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times L^{2}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R})\,,

where the
A
,
\widetilde{A}
operators map into
L^{2}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R})
and the
B
,
\widetilde{B}
operators map into
H^{1}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R})
. Therefore, we write

(6.51)

P=\begin{bmatrix}(1+\partial_{s}^{4})^{-1}&&&\\
&(1+\partial_{s}^{4})^{-1}&&\\
&&1&\\
&&&(1-\frac{\eta}{\alpha}\partial_{s}^{2})^{-1}\end{bmatrix}\,,

(6.52)

\displaystyle P\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}

\displaystyle={\rm Id}+P\begin{bmatrix}0&&&-\eta(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss})\cdot\bm{e}_{1}\partial_{ss}\\
&0&&-\eta(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss})\cdot\bm{e}_{2}\partial_{ss}\\
&&0&\\
&&&0\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle+P\begin{bmatrix}-1+\widetilde{A}_{11}&A_{12}&&\widetilde{A}_{14}\\
A_{21}&-1+\widetilde{A}_{22}&&\widetilde{A}_{24}\\
&&-1&\\
B_{41}&B_{42}&&-1+\widetilde{B}_{44}\end{bmatrix}\,,

i.e.,
P\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}={\rm Id}+\text{ strictly upper triangular }+\text{ compact}:H^{3}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R})^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times H^{2}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R})\circlearrowleft
,
which is a compact perturbation of an invertible operator and hence Fredholm index zero.

Finally, when
\gamma\geq 0
, we argue as before by appealing to equivalent norms, as in (
6.18
).
∎

7.
Further investigations

7.1.
Vanishing rotational friction coefficient

We next consider the closed rod in the limit as
\alpha\to 0
and prove Theorem
1.4
, global well-posedness without restriction on
\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}
. Expressing the closed curve and frame system (
7.1
)-(
7.2
) as
\alpha\to 0
in terms of
(\bm{X}_{s},\overline{\kappa}_{3})
, we have

(7.1)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}+(\bm{X}_{s})_{ssss}

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{R}^{0}[\bm{X}_{s}])_{s}

(7.2)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\overline{\kappa}_{3}

\displaystyle=\overline{\mathcal{R}}(t)\,,

with

(7.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{0}[\bm{X}_{s}]

\displaystyle=(1+\gamma)\lambda_{s}\bm{X}_{s}+\lambda\bm{X}_{ss}+\eta\overline{\kappa}_{3}(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{sss})+3\gamma\bm{X}_{s}(\bm{X}_{ss}\cdot\bm{X}_{sss})

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{R}}(t)

\displaystyle=\eta\overline{\kappa}_{3}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\bigg(-\left\lvert\bm{X}_{sss}\right\rvert^{2}+\left\lvert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rvert^{4}\bigg)\,ds

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}}\bigg(\bm{X}_{ssss}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{sss})+\lambda\bm{X}_{sss}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss})\bigg)\,ds\,.

The tension
\lambda
satisfies

(7.4)

(1+\gamma)\lambda_{ss}-\left\lvert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rvert^{2}\lambda=-(4+3\gamma)(\bm{X}_{sss}\cdot\bm{X}_{ss})_{s}+\left\lvert\bm{X}_{sss}\right\rvert^{2}-\eta\overline{\kappa}_{3}\bm{X}_{s}\cdot(\bm{X}_{ss}\times\bm{X}_{sss})\,.

Since
\overline{\kappa}_{3}
is constant in
s
, local well-posedness for
(\bm{X}_{s},\overline{\kappa}_{3})\in\big(C([0,T];H^{1}_{s})\cap L^{2}_{t}H^{3}_{s}\big)\times C([0,T])
follows from analysis similar to Section
3
, and the solution can be continued provided that
\bm{X}_{s}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{s}\cap L^{2}_{t}H^{3}_{s}(\mathbb{T}\times(0,T))
and
\overline{\kappa}_{3}
remains bounded, which we verify below.

To obtain global well-posedness, we again turn to energy methods. We now have

(7.5)

\widetilde{\bm{Z}}=\bm{X}_{sss}-\lambda\bm{X}_{s}-\eta\overline{\kappa}_{3}\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss}\,,

and the analogous energy and dissipation quantities are given by

(7.6)

E(t)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\bigg(\left\lvert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rvert^{2}+\eta\overline{\kappa}_{3}^{2}\bigg)\,ds\,,\quad D(t)=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\big(({\bf I}+\gamma\bm{X}_{s}\otimes\bm{X}_{s})\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\big)\cdot\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{s}\,ds\,.

The system (
7.1
)-(
7.2
) then satisfies the energy identity

(7.7)

\displaystyle\frac{dE}{dt}=-D(t)\,.

Recall that the size restriction on
\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}}
in the
\alpha>0
case stemmed from needing to bound
\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}
by
D(t)
and
E(t)
. In the
\alpha\to 0
case, we may attain such a bound without the size restriction. In particular, as in the
\alpha>0
case, we define

(7.8)

\displaystyle D_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\bigg(\left\lvert\bm{Z}_{s}\right\rvert^{2}+\gamma\big(\bm{X}_{s}\cdot\bm{Z}_{s}\big)^{2}\bigg)\,ds\,.

where again
\bm{Z}=\bm{X}_{sss}-\lambda\bm{X}_{s}
. Again we have

(7.9)

\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lesssim D_{1}(t)^{1/2}(1+E(t)^{1/2})+E(t)+E(t)^{5/2}

by (
3.69
). We then have

(7.10)

D_{1}(t)+\eta^{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lvert\overline{\kappa}_{3}\big(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss}\big)_{s}\right\rvert^{2}\,ds\leq D(t)+2\eta\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lvert\overline{\kappa}_{3}\bm{Z}_{s}\cdot\big(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss}\big)_{s}\right\rvert\,ds\,.

Splitting up the right-hand side as in (
5.32
), we still have a term to estimate:

(7.11)

\displaystyle\eta^{2}\overline{\kappa}_{3}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lvert\bm{X}_{sss}\right\rvert^{2}\,ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\overline{\kappa}_{3}^{2}\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim D_{1}(t)^{1/2}(E(t)^{3/2}+E(t)^{2})+E(t)^{5/2}+E(t)^{4}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon D_{1}(t)+C(E,\varepsilon)\,.

Taking
\varepsilon=1/4
, we may absorb
D_{1}(t)
into the left-hand side of (
7.10
) to obtain

(7.12)

D_{1}(t)\leq 4D(t)+C(E)\,,\qquad\lVert\bm{X}_{ssss}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s}}\leq C(E)D(t)+C(E)\,.

This verifies that
\bm{X}_{s}\in L^{2}_{t}H^{3}_{s}(I\times(0,T))
on any finite interval, and hence the solution can be continued indefinitely.

7.2.
On unconditional curve-frame well-posedness

Despite the condition (
1.14
) on the maximum filament curvature required in our proof of Theorem
1.3
, we have reason to suspect that the coupled curve-frame evolution (
1.10
)-(
1.11
) is in fact well posed without such a condition.
In particular, the apparent backward heat evolution may not actually be present, as it is cancelled by the strong coupling between (
1.10
) and (
1.11
).
We will use the
\kappa_{j}
formulation (
2.33
)-(
2.35
) with
\zeta_{1}=\zeta_{2}=\zeta_{3}=0
and consider the linearized curve-frame evolution for a closed filament about the multiply-covered covered circle with constant (very large) curvature
\overline{\kappa}
and constant twist rate
b
. For this state, the filament tension is constant and satisfies
\lambda_{0}=(1-\eta)b^{2}
. If
b=0
, this is a steady state of the equations.

Linearizing (
2.33
)-(
2.35
) about
(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\kappa_{3})=(\overline{\kappa},0,b)
for
\overline{\kappa},b
constant, we obtain the system

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\kappa_{1}

\displaystyle=-(\kappa_{1})_{ssss}+\big((5-2\eta)b^{2}-(1+\gamma)\overline{\kappa}^{2}\big)(\kappa_{1})_{ss}+(4-\eta)b(\kappa_{2})_{sss}

(7.13)

\displaystyle\quad-(2-\eta)b^{3}(\kappa_{2})_{s}+(4-4\eta)b\overline{\kappa}(\kappa_{3})_{ss}-2(1-\eta)\overline{\kappa}b^{3}\kappa_{3}+\overline{\kappa}b^{2}\widetilde{\lambda}+\gamma\overline{\kappa}\widetilde{\lambda}_{ss}\,,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\kappa_{2}

\displaystyle=-(\kappa_{2})_{ssss}+(5-2\eta)b^{2}(\kappa_{2})_{ss}+\big((2-\eta)b^{3}-(1+\gamma)b\overline{\kappa}^{2}\big)(\kappa_{1})_{s}

\displaystyle\quad-(4-\eta)b(\kappa_{1})_{sss}-(1-\eta)\overline{\kappa}(\kappa_{3})_{sss}

(7.14)

\displaystyle\quad+\overline{\kappa}\bigg(5(1-\eta)b^{2}-\frac{\eta}{\alpha}\bigg)(\kappa_{3})_{s}-(1-\gamma)\overline{\kappa}b\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}\,,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\kappa_{3}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{\eta}{\alpha}-(1-\eta)\overline{\kappa}^{2}\bigg)(\kappa_{3})_{ss}+2(1-\eta)\overline{\kappa}^{2}b^{2}\kappa_{3}-\overline{\kappa}(\kappa_{2})_{sss}

(7.15)

\displaystyle\quad+(2-\eta)b^{2}\overline{\kappa}(\kappa_{2})_{s}-(3-\eta)b\overline{\kappa}(\kappa_{1})_{ss}-b\overline{\kappa}^{2}\widetilde{\lambda}\,,

along with the linearized tension equation (where here
\widetilde{\lambda}=-(\lambda+\kappa^{2})=-(\lambda+\kappa_{1}^{2})
, as in
[
85
]
)

(7.16)

(1+\gamma)\widetilde{\lambda}_{ss}-\overline{\kappa}^{2}\widetilde{\lambda}=(2+\gamma)\overline{\kappa}(\kappa_{1})_{ss}-(2-\eta)b\overline{\kappa}(\kappa_{2})_{s}+2(\eta-1)\overline{\kappa}^{2}b\kappa_{3}\,.

Note that if
b=0
, the
\kappa_{1}
evolution completely decouples from the
(\kappa_{2},\kappa_{3})
evolution:

(7.17)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\kappa_{1}

\displaystyle=-(\kappa_{1})_{ssss}-(1+\gamma)\overline{\kappa}^{2}(\kappa_{1})_{ss}+\gamma\overline{\kappa}\widetilde{\lambda}_{ss}\,,

(7.18)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\kappa_{2}

\displaystyle=-(\kappa_{2})_{ssss}-(1-\eta)\overline{\kappa}(\kappa_{3})_{sss}-\frac{\eta}{\alpha}\overline{\kappa}(\kappa_{3})_{s}\,,

(7.19)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\kappa_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta}{\alpha}(\kappa_{3})_{ss}+(\eta-1)\overline{\kappa}^{2}(\kappa_{3})_{ss}-\overline{\kappa}(\kappa_{2})_{sss}\,,

where

(7.20)

(1+\gamma)\widetilde{\lambda}_{ss}-\overline{\kappa}^{2}\widetilde{\lambda}=(2+\gamma)\overline{\kappa}(\kappa_{1})_{ss}\,.

Expanding each
\kappa_{j}(s,t)
,
j=1,2,3
, and
\widetilde{\lambda}(s,t)
as Fourier series in
s
,

(7.21)

\kappa_{j}(s,t)=\sum_{n=-\infty}^{\infty}e^{i2\pi ns}\widehat{\kappa}_{j}(n,t)\,,\qquad\widetilde{\lambda}(s,t)=\sum_{n=-\infty}^{\infty}e^{i2\pi ns}\widehat{\lambda}(n,t)\,,

we may solve for the tension coefficients
\widehat{\lambda}(n,t)
as

(7.22)

\widehat{\lambda}=\frac{(2\pi n)^{2}(2+\gamma)\overline{\kappa}}{(1+\gamma)(2\pi n)^{2}+\overline{\kappa}^{2}}\widehat{\kappa}_{1}+\frac{(i2\pi n)(2-\eta)b\overline{\kappa}}{(1+\gamma)(2\pi n)^{2}+\overline{\kappa}^{2}}\widehat{\kappa}_{2}-\frac{2(\eta-1)\overline{\kappa}^{2}b}{(1+\gamma)(2\pi n)^{2}+\overline{\kappa}^{2}}\widehat{\kappa}_{3}\,.

The coefficients
\widehat{\kappa}_{j}(n,t)
then satisfy the following system of ODEs:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\widehat{\kappa}_{1}

\displaystyle=-(2\pi n)^{2}\bigg((2\pi n)^{2}+(5-2\eta)b^{2}-(1+\gamma)\overline{\kappa}^{2}+\frac{(2+\gamma)\overline{\kappa}^{2}((2\pi n)^{2}\gamma-b^{2})}{(1+\gamma)(2\pi n)^{2}+\overline{\kappa}^{2}}\bigg)\widehat{\kappa}_{1}

(7.23)

\displaystyle\quad-i(2\pi n)\bigg((2\pi n)^{2}(4-\eta)b+(2-\eta)b^{3}+\frac{(2-\eta)b\overline{\kappa}^{2}((2\pi n)^{2}\gamma-b^{2})}{(1+\gamma)(2\pi n)^{2}+\overline{\kappa}^{2}}\bigg)\widehat{\kappa}_{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\bigg((2\pi n)^{2}(4-4\eta)b\overline{\kappa}+2(1-\eta)\overline{\kappa}b^{3}+\frac{2(1-\eta)\overline{\kappa}^{3}b((2\pi n)^{2}\gamma-b^{2})}{(1+\gamma)(2\pi n)^{2}+\overline{\kappa}^{2}}\bigg)\widehat{\kappa}_{3}

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\widehat{\kappa}_{2}

\displaystyle=i(2\pi n)b\bigg((2\pi n)^{2}(4-\eta)+(2-\eta)b^{2}-(1+\gamma)\overline{\kappa}^{2}-\frac{(2\pi n)^{2}(1-\gamma)(2+\gamma)\overline{\kappa}^{2}}{(1+\gamma)(2\pi n)^{2}+\overline{\kappa}^{2}}\bigg)\widehat{\kappa}_{1}

(7.24)

\displaystyle\quad-(2\pi n)^{2}\bigg((2\pi n)^{2}+(5-2\eta)b^{2}-\frac{(1-\gamma)(2-\eta)\overline{\kappa}^{2}b^{2}}{(1+\gamma)(2\pi n)^{2}+\overline{\kappa}^{2}}\bigg)\widehat{\kappa}_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+i(2\pi n)\bigg((2\pi n)^{2}(1-\eta)\overline{\kappa}+(5-5\eta)\overline{\kappa}b^{2}-\frac{\eta}{\alpha}\overline{\kappa}-\frac{2(1-\gamma)(1-\eta)\overline{\kappa}^{3}b^{2}}{(1+\gamma)(2\pi n)^{2}+\overline{\kappa}^{2}}\bigg)\widehat{\kappa}_{3}

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\widehat{\kappa}_{3}

\displaystyle=(2\pi n)^{2}\bigg((3-\eta)b\overline{\kappa}-\frac{(2+\gamma)b\overline{\kappa}^{3}}{(1+\gamma)(2\pi n)^{2}+\overline{\kappa}^{2}}\bigg)\widehat{\kappa}_{1}

(7.25)

\displaystyle\quad+i(2\pi n)\bigg((2\pi n)^{2}\overline{\kappa}+(2-\eta)b^{2}\overline{\kappa}-\frac{(2-\eta)b^{2}\overline{\kappa}^{3}}{(1+\gamma)(2\pi n)^{2}+\overline{\kappa}^{2}}\bigg)\widehat{\kappa}_{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\bigg((2\pi n)^{2}\bigg(\frac{\eta}{\alpha}-(1-\eta)\overline{\kappa}^{2}\bigg)-2(1-\eta)\overline{\kappa}^{2}b^{2}-\frac{2(\eta-1)b^{2}\overline{\kappa}^{4}}{(1+\gamma)(2\pi n)^{2}+\overline{\kappa}^{2}}\bigg)\widehat{\kappa}_{3}\,.

As
n\to\infty
, the leading order asymptotic behavior of the system (
7.23
)-(
7.25
) is given by

(7.26)

\partial_{t}\begin{pmatrix}\widehat{\kappa}_{1}\\
\widehat{\kappa}_{2}\\
\widehat{\kappa}_{3}\end{pmatrix}\sim-(2\pi n)^{2}\begin{pmatrix}(2\pi n)^{2}&i(2\pi n)(4-\eta)b&4(1-\eta)b\overline{\kappa}\\
-i(2\pi n)(4-\eta)b&(2\pi n)^{2}&-i(2\pi n)(1-\eta)\overline{\kappa}\\
-(3-\eta)b\overline{\kappa}&-i(2\pi n)\overline{\kappa}&\frac{\eta}{\alpha}-(1-\eta)\overline{\kappa}^{2}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\widehat{\kappa}_{1}\\
\widehat{\kappa}_{2}\\
\widehat{\kappa}_{3}\end{pmatrix}\,.

We may calculate the eigenvalues
\mu_{1}(n),\mu_{2}(n),\mu_{3}(n)
of the above matrix, which, as
n\to\infty
, satisfy

(7.27)

\mu_{1}\,,\;\mu_{2}=-(2\pi n)^{4}+O(n^{3})\,,\quad\mu_{3}=-(2\pi n)^{2}\frac{\eta}{\alpha}+O(n)\,,\qquad n\to\infty\,.

In particular, no matter how large
\overline{\kappa}
is, all
\mu_{j}
are negative beyond some finite wavenumber
n
. This suggests that the backward heat operator apparently present in (
1.13
) for large initial curvatures is not really there; in fact, the strong coupling between the curve and frame evolutions actually helps counteract the development of backward heat.

To see the effect of the strong coupling more explicitly, we consider the
b=0
setting. The system (
7.23
)-(
7.25
) then simplifies to

(7.28)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\widehat{\kappa}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{(2\pi)^{2}(1+\gamma)n^{2}}{(2\pi)^{2}(1+\gamma)n^{2}+\overline{\kappa}^{2}}\big(-(2\pi)^{4}n^{4}+\overline{\kappa}^{4}\big)\widehat{\kappa}_{1}\,,

(7.29)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\begin{pmatrix}\widehat{\kappa}_{2}\\
\widehat{\kappa}_{3}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}-(2\pi n)^{4}&-i2\pi n\overline{\kappa}\big((2\pi n)^{2}(\eta-1)+\frac{\eta}{\alpha}\big)\\
i(2\pi n)^{3}\overline{\kappa}&-(2\pi n)^{2}\big(\frac{\eta}{\alpha}+(\eta-1)\overline{\kappa}^{2}\big)\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\widehat{\kappa}_{2}\\
\widehat{\kappa}_{3}\end{pmatrix}\,.

We may explicitly write down the eigenvalues of the matrix in (
7.29
) as

(7.30)

\displaystyle\mu_{2}\,,\mu_{3}

\displaystyle=-\frac{(2\pi n)^{2}}{2}\bigg[(2\pi n)^{2}+\frac{\eta}{\alpha}+(\eta-1)\overline{\kappa}^{2}

\displaystyle\pm\sqrt{\bigg((2\pi n)^{2}-\bigg(\frac{\eta}{\alpha}+(\eta-1)\overline{\kappa}^{2}\bigg)\bigg)^{2}+4\overline{\kappa}^{2}\bigg(\frac{\eta}{\alpha}+(2\pi n)^{2}(\eta-1)\bigg)}\bigg]

\displaystyle=-\frac{(2\pi n)^{2}}{2}\bigg[(2\pi n)^{2}+\frac{\eta}{\alpha}+(\eta-1)\overline{\kappa}^{2}

\displaystyle\pm\sqrt{\bigg((2\pi n)^{2}+\frac{\eta}{\alpha}+(\eta-1)\overline{\kappa}^{2}\bigg)^{2}+4\frac{\eta}{\alpha}\big(\overline{\kappa}^{2}-(2\pi n)^{2}\big)}\bigg]\,.

As
n\to\infty
, we obtain the same leading order behavior as (
7.27
). Here, however, we can see more precisely how the strong coupling between the
\kappa_{2}
and
\kappa_{3}
equations exactly cancels the backward heat part
(2\pi n)^{2}\overline{\kappa}^{2}(\eta-1)
appearing in (
7.29
).

Our aim with the above spectral calculations is to highlight the possible regularizing role of the strong curve-frame coupling, and in particular, the apparent cancellation of the backward heat behavior through this coupling. Although our focus here is on the asymptotic spectral behavior as
n\to\infty
, we note that there are a variety of low wavenumber instabilities that are interesting in their own right. For the case
b=0
above, we can see from the expression (
7.30
) that for large enough
\overline{\kappa}
,
\mu_{3}
is positive for small
n
, so the multiply-covered circle is linearly unstable to long-wave perturbations. This is related to the well-studied Michell’s instability
[
76
,
30
,
111
,
3
]
, which arises due to the balance between the bending and twisting energy of the rod. For Michell’s instability, a build-up of twist energy in a closed planar ring is relieved by deformation, i.e., releasing some of the extra twist energy into bending. Here we see the analogous phenomenon: a build-up of bending energy may be relieved by twisting the rod.

Appendix A
Hyperviscous regularization

We consider (
5.1
)-(
5.4
) with an additional hyperviscous term in the
\kappa_{3}
equation:

(A.1)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bm{X}_{s}+(\bm{X}_{s})_{ssss}

\displaystyle=\big(\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{1}[\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3}]\big)_{s}+\big(\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{2}[\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3}]\big)_{ss}

(A.2)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\kappa_{3}+\nu(\kappa_{3})_{ssss}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{0}[\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3}]+\big(\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{1}[\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3}]\big)_{s}+\big(\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{2}[\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3}]\big)_{ss}\,.

Here
\nu>0
is a parameter that we will eventually send to zero. The remainder terms are given by

(A.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{1}[\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3}]

\displaystyle=-\gamma\lambda\bm{X}_{ss}+3\gamma\bm{X}_{s}(\bm{X}_{ss}\cdot\bm{X}_{sss})

(A.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{2}[\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3}]

\displaystyle=(1+\gamma)\lambda\bm{X}_{s}+\eta\kappa_{3}\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{0}[\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3}]

\displaystyle=-2\eta\bm{X}_{ss}\cdot\bm{X}_{sss}(\kappa_{3})_{s}-\eta\kappa_{3}\bm{X}_{ssss}\cdot\bm{X}_{ss}-2\eta\kappa_{3}\left\lvert\bm{X}_{sss}\right\rvert^{2}

(A.5)

\displaystyle\quad+\eta\kappa_{3}\left\lvert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rvert^{4}+\bm{X}_{ssss}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{sss})+\lambda\bm{X}_{sss}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss})

(A.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{1}[\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3}]

\displaystyle=-\bm{X}_{ssss}\cdot(\bm{X}_{s}\times\bm{X}_{ss})

(A.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{2}[\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3}]

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{\eta}{\alpha}+\eta\left\lvert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rvert^{2}\bigg)\kappa_{3}\,.

The filament tension
\lambda
continues to satisfy (
5.4
), which we rewrite here for convenience:

(A.8)

(1+\gamma)\lambda_{ss}-\left\lvert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rvert^{2}\lambda=-(4+3\gamma)(\bm{X}_{sss}\cdot\bm{X}_{ss})_{s}+\left\lvert\bm{X}_{sss}\right\rvert^{2}-\eta\kappa_{3}\bm{X}_{s}\cdot(\bm{X}_{ss}\times\bm{X}_{sss})\,.

We show the following qualitative existence and uniqueness result.

Lemma A.1

(Qualitative short-time theory with hyperviscosity)
.

Let

(A.9)

\bm{X}_{s}^{\rm in}\in H^{3}_{s}\,,\quad|\bm{X}_{s}^{\rm in}|^{2}=1\,,\quad\kappa_{3}^{\rm in}\in H^{1}_{s}\,,

satisfying the boundary conditions, and consider (
A.1
)-(
A.2
) for some
\nu>0
. Then there exists
T=T(\bm{X}^{\rm in},\kappa_{3}^{\rm in},\nu)>0
and a solution

(A.10)

\bm{X}_{s}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{3}_{s}\cap L^{2}_{t}H^{5}_{s}\,,\quad\kappa_{3}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{s}\cap L^{2}_{t}H^{3}_{s}

to the hyperviscous problem. The solution is unique in this class and can be extended to a maximal solution provided the norms do not blow up.

Proof of Lemma
A.1
.

We will consider the pair
(\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3})
belonging to the space

(A.11)

\mathcal{Y}=(L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{s}\cap L^{2}_{t}H^{4}_{s})\times(L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{s}\cap L^{2}_{t}H^{3}_{s})\cap\{{\rm BCs}\}\,,

equipped with the norm

(A.12)

\lVert(\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3})\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}}:=\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}H^{4}_{s}}+\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}+\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}H^{3}_{s}}\,.

The space
\mathcal{Y}
does not fall exactly into the energy estimates in Section
3.1
, and we instead appeal to the estimates in Lemma
A.2
.
To close a fixed point argument for
(\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3})
in
\mathcal{Y}
, we will need bounds for
\lVert\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{1}\rVert_{L^{4/3}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}
,
\lVert\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{2}\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}
,
\|\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{0}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}
,
\|\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{0}\|_{L^{4/3}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}
, and
\|\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}
. We will not explicitly track the dependence of these estimates on the parameter
\nu
as
\nu\to 0
.

Stabilizes a ball
. We begin by estimating the tension
\lambda
in terms of
\bm{X}_{ss}
and
\kappa_{3}
. By analogous arguments to Lemma
3.3
using the form of (
A.8
), we obtain

(A.13)

\lVert\lambda\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{2}(1+\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}})\,.

We may also obtain the
H^{2}_{s}
bound

(A.14)

\lVert\lambda\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}\lesssim(\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{4}_{s}}^{1/2}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{3/2}+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{4})(1+\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}})

directly from the equation (
A.8
).
Using the tension estimates (
A.13
) and (
A.14
), we may obtain the following
H^{2}_{s}
bounds for
\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{1}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{2}
:

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{1}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert\lambda\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}+\lVert\lambda\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{1/2}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{4}_{s}}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{5/2}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{4}_{s}}^{1/2}+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{4}_{s}}

(A.15)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{4}_{s}}^{1/2}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{5/2}+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{5})(1+\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}})+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{4}_{s}}\,,

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{2}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert\lambda\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}+\lVert\lambda\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}+\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{H^{3}_{s}}^{1/2}\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}^{1/2}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle\quad+\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{2}+\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{4}_{s}}^{1/2}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{1/2}

(A.16)

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{4}_{s}}^{1/2}(\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{3/2}+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{1/2})+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{4}\big)(1+\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}})

\displaystyle\quad+\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{H^{3}_{s}}^{1/2}\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}^{1/2}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}\,.

We may further obtain the following
H^{1}_{s}
bounds for
\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{0}
,
\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{1}
, and
\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{2}
:

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{0}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{4}_{s}}(1+\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}})+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{2}\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}^{1/2}\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{H^{3}_{s}}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{4}+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{4}_{s}}^{1/2}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{5/2}+\lVert\lambda\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{3/2}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{4}_{s}}^{1/2}

(A.17)

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{4}_{s}}+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{4}_{s}}^{1/2}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{7/2}\big)(1+\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}})

\displaystyle\quad+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{2}\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}^{1/2}\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{H^{3}_{s}}^{1/2}\,,

(A.18)

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{1}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{4}_{s}}\,,

(A.19)

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{2}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}(1+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{2})\,.

Let
S_{T}(\bm{X}_{ss},\kappa_{3})
denote the time-
T
solution map for the system (
A.1
)-(
A.2
). We may now show that
S_{T}(\cdot,\cdot)
maps a ball to a ball in
\mathcal{Y}
. Let
\lVert(\bm{X}_{ss},\kappa_{3})\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}}\leq N
for some
N>0
.
Integrating the inequalities (
A.15
), (
A.16
), (
A.17
), (
A.18
), and (
A.19
) in time from 0 to
T
, we may estimate

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{1}\rVert_{L^{4/3}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(T^{1/2}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}H^{4}_{s}}^{1/2}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}^{5/2}+T^{3/4}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}^{5})(1+\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{s}})

\displaystyle\quad+T^{1/4}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}H^{4}_{s}}

(A.20)

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{1/2}N^{3}(1+T^{1/4}N^{2})(1+N)+T^{1/4}N^{2}\,,

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{2}\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{s}})\big(T^{1/4}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}H^{4}_{s}}^{1/2}(\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}^{3/2}+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}^{1/2})

\displaystyle\quad+T^{1/2}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}^{4}\big)+T^{1/4}\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}H^{3}_{s}}^{1/2}\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}^{1/2}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}

(A.21)

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{1/4}N\big(1+N+T^{1/4}N^{3}\big)(1+N)\,,

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{0}\rVert_{L^{1}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{s}})\big(T^{1/2}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}H^{4}_{s}}

\displaystyle\quad+T^{3/4}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}H^{4}_{s}}^{1/2}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}^{7/2}\big)+T^{3/4}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}^{2}\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}^{1/2}\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}H^{3}_{s}}^{1/2}

(A.22)

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{1/2}N^{2}\big(1+T^{1/4}N^{2}\big)(1+N)+T^{3/4}N^{3}\,,

(A.23)

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{1}\rVert_{L^{4/3}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{1/4}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}H^{4}_{s}}\lesssim T^{1/4}N^{2}\,,

(A.24)

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{2}\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{1/2}\lVert\kappa_{3}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}(1+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}^{2})\lesssim T^{1/2}N(1+N^{2})\,.

Using the form of the estimates in Lemma
A.2
for (
A.1
) and (
A.2
), we thus obtain

(A.25)

\displaystyle\lVert S_{T}(\bm{X}_{ss},\kappa_{3})\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}}

\displaystyle\leq\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{\rm in}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}+\lVert\kappa_{3}^{\rm in}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}+C(\lVert\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{1}\rVert_{L^{4/3}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}+\lVert\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{2}\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}H^{2}_{s}})

\displaystyle+C(\lVert\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{0}\rVert_{L^{1}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}+\lVert\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{1}\rVert_{L^{4/3}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}+\lVert\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{2}\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}H^{1}_{s}})

\displaystyle\leq\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{\rm in}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}+\lVert\kappa_{3}^{\rm in}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}

\displaystyle+C\,T^{1/4}\,N\big(1+N^{2}+T^{1/4}N^{2}(1+T^{1/4}N^{2})(1+N^{4})\big)\,.

Choosing
N=2(\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{\rm in}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}+\lVert\kappa_{3}^{\rm in}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}})
and taking
T
small enough that
C\,T^{1/4}\,N\big(1+N^{2}+T^{1/4}N^{2}(1+T^{1/4}N^{2})(1+N^{4})\big)\leq\frac{N}{4}
, we obtain

(A.26)

\lVert S_{T}(\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3})\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}}\leq\frac{3}{4}N\,.

Contraction
. We next show that the solution map is a contraction on the ball of radius
N
in
\mathcal{Y}
. Consider two curve-frame pairs
(\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)},\kappa_{3}^{(1)})
and
(\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)},\kappa_{3}^{(2)})
belonging to this ball. We again begin with the tension, and again follow the curve-only setting. As in (
3.42
), we let
R_{\lambda}^{(k)}
denote the right-hand side of the tension equation (
A.8
) for curve-frame pair
(\bm{X}_{s}^{(k)},\kappa_{3}^{(k)})
,
k=1,2
. We may estimate the difference

(A.27)

\displaystyle\lVert R_{\lambda}^{(1)}-R_{\lambda}^{(2)}\rVert_{(H^{1})^{*}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}\big(1+(1+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}})\|\kappa_{3}^{(2)}\|_{H^{1}_{s}}\big)\times

\displaystyle\times\big(\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}\big)+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{2}\|\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)}\|_{H^{1}_{s}}\,.

Defining
q^{(1)}
,
q^{(2)}
as in (
3.45
), by analogous arguments, we obtain the estimate

(A.28)

\displaystyle\lVert q^{(1)}-q^{(2)}\rVert_{H^{1}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}\big)\times

\displaystyle\times\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}\rVert_{H^{2}_{s}}^{2}(1+\|\kappa_{3}^{(1)}\|_{H^{1}_{s}})\,.

Combining (
A.27
) and (
A.28
), we may then obtain the following bound for
\lambda^{(1)}-\lambda^{(2)}
on
\mathcal{Y}
:

(A.29)

\displaystyle\lVert\lambda^{(1)}-\lambda^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}\lesssim N(1+N^{3})\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}+N^{2}\|\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}\,.

From the tension equation (
A.8
) and the
L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{s}
bound (
A.29
), we may additionally obtain the following
L^{2}_{t}H^{2}_{s}
estimate for
\lambda^{(1)}-\lambda^{(2)}
on
\mathcal{Y}
:

(A.30)

\displaystyle\lVert\lambda^{(1)}-\lambda^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{1/4}\,N\bigg(\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}H^{4}_{s}}^{1/2}\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}\bigg)+T^{1/2}\,N^{2}\bigg((1+N^{2})\|\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}

\displaystyle+\big(1+N^{4}\big)\lVert\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}\bigg)\,.

Furthermore, each of the right-hand side differences
(\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{j})^{(1)}-(\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{j})^{(2)}
and
(\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{j})^{(1)}-(\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{j})^{(2)}
may be shown to satisfy

(A.31)

\displaystyle\lVert(\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{1})^{(1)}-(\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{1})^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{4/3}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}\lesssim T^{1/4}N\lVert\lambda^{(1)}-\lambda^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}+T^{1/2}N\lVert\lambda^{(1)}-\lambda^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}

\displaystyle+T^{1/4}N\big(1+T^{1/4}N(1+T^{1/4}N^{2})(1+N)\big)\lVert(\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)},\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}}\,,

\displaystyle\lVert(\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{2})^{(1)}-(\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{2})^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert\lambda^{(1)}-\lambda^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}H^{2}_{s}}+T^{1/2}N\lVert\lambda^{(1)}-\lambda^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}

\displaystyle+T^{1/4}N\big(1+T^{1/4}(1+N^{2})\big)(1+N^{2})\lVert(\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)},\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}}\,,

\displaystyle\lVert(\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{0})^{(1)}-(\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{0})^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{1}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}\lesssim T^{1/2}\big(1+T^{1/4}N\big)(N+N^{3})\lVert(\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)},\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}}\,,

\displaystyle\lVert(\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{1})^{(1)}-(\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{1})^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{4/3}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}\lesssim T^{1/4}N\lVert(\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)},\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}}\,,

\displaystyle\lVert(\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{2})^{(1)}-(\mathcal{R}^{\rm f}_{2})^{(2)}\rVert_{L^{2}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}\lesssim T^{1/2}(1+N^{2})\lVert(\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)},\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}}\,.

Altogether, using Lemma
A.2
, we have

(A.32)

\displaystyle\lVert S_{T}(\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)},\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(T^{1/4}(N+N^{3})+T^{1/2}(1+N^{6})

\displaystyle+T^{3/4}(N^{2}+N^{7})\big)\lVert(\bm{X}_{s}^{(1)}-\bm{X}_{s}^{(2)},\kappa_{3}^{(1)}-\kappa_{3}^{(2)})\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}}\,.

Taking
T
small enough that
C\big(T^{1/4}(N+N^{3})+T^{1/2}(1+N^{6})+T^{3/4}(N^{2}+N^{7})\big)<1
, we obtain a contraction on
\mathcal{Y}
, and hence the existence of a unique solution
(\bm{X}_{s},\kappa_{3})\in(L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{s}\cap L^{2}_{t}H^{4}_{s})\times(L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{s}\cap L^{2}_{t}H^{3}_{s})
.

Bootstrapping
. We next apply a bootstrapping argument to demonstrate, qualitatively, that

(A.33)

\bm{X}_{s}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{3}_{s}\cap L^{2}_{t}H^{5}_{s}(I\times(0,T))\,.

This will be enough to justify the quantitative energy estimates in Section
5
. We again consider the
\bm{X}_{s}
equation (
A.1
). According to Lemma
3.1
, (
A.33
) will hold provided that
(\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{1})_{s},(\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{2})_{ss}\in L^{2}_{t}H^{1}_{s}
. It suffices to verify
\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{1}\in L^{2}_{t}H^{2}_{s}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\rm c}_{2}\in L^{2}_{t}H^{3}_{s}
based on our current information that
\bm{X}_{s}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{s}\cap L^{2}_{t}H^{4}_{s}
and
\kappa_{3}\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{s}\cap L^{2}_{t}H^{3}_{s}
. Throughout, we rely on the tame estimates

(A.34)

\|fg\|_{H^{k}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}\|g\|_{H^{k}}+\|f\|_{H^{k}}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}}

and 1D Sobolev embedding into
L^{\infty}
. The main non-trivial point to verify is that
\lambda\in L^{2}_{t}H^{3}_{s}
. For this, we examine the equation

(A.35)

(1+\gamma)\lambda_{ss}=|\bm{X}_{ss}|^{2}\lambda-(4+3\gamma)(\bm{X}_{sss}\cdot\bm{X}_{ss})_{s}+|\bm{X}_{sss}|^{2}-\eta\kappa_{3}\bm{X}_{s}(\bm{X}_{ss}\times\bm{X}_{sss})\,.

Under the current assumptions, the right-hand side belongs to
L^{2}_{t}H^{1}_{s}
. This completes the bootstrapping argument.
∎

A.1.
Parabolic estimates, revisited

We prove an extension of Lemma
3.1
.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
T>0
and
I=[0,1]
or
\mathbb{T}
. Suppose
u^{\rm in}\in H^{1}_{0}
,
f_{0}\in L^{1}_{t}(H^{1}_{0})_{s}
,
f_{1}\in L^{4/3}_{t}H^{1}_{s}
, and
f_{2}\in L^{2}_{t}H^{1}_{s}
(with norms evaluated on
I\times(0,T)
). Then the solution
u
to the Dirichlet biharmonic problem

(A.36)

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+\partial_{s}^{4})u

\displaystyle=f_{0}+\partial_{s}f_{1}+\partial_{s}^{2}f_{2}

\displaystyle u\,,\,\partial_{s}u\big|_{\partial I}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle u\big|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=u^{\rm in}

belongs to
C([0,T];H^{1}_{s})\cap L^{2}_{t}H^{3}_{s}
, and

(A.37)

\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}+\|\partial_{s}^{2}u\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{L^{1}_{t}(H^{1}_{0})_{s}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{4/3}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}+\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{1}_{s}}\,.

Proof.

We concern ourselves only with
I=[0,1]
, since the periodic estimates can be justified on the basis of Fourier series. Consider the unbounded operator
L:D(L)\subset L^{2}_{s}\to L^{2}_{s}
defined by

(A.38)

L=\partial_{s}^{4}\,,\quad D(L):=\{H^{4}_{s}(I):u,\partial_{s}u|_{s=0,1}=0\}\,.

Then
L
is positive and self-adjoint, and
-L
generates an analytic semigroup. The eigenvalues
\lambda_{k}
and
L^{2}_{s}
-normalized eigenfunctions
\phi_{k}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
, were computed in
[
81
]
. The fractional powers of
L
are expressed in terms of the eigenfunction expansions

(A.39)

L^{\theta}u=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\lambda_{k}^{\theta}\langle u_{k},\phi_{k}\rangle\phi_{k}(s)\,,

where
\theta\geq 0
and
u\in\mathcal{D}(L^{\theta})
consists of those
u\in L^{2}
satisfying
(\lambda_{k}^{\theta}|\langle u_{k},\phi_{k}\rangle|)_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\in\ell^{2}(\mathbb{N})
. Crucially, we have the following characterization of the domains of fractional powers of the operators
[
37
]
:

(A.40)

\mathcal{D}(L^{\theta}):=H^{\sigma}\cap H^{\min(\sigma,2)}_{0}\,,\quad\forall\sigma=4\theta\in[0,4]\,,

with the norm equivalence

(A.41)

\|u\|_{H^{\sigma}_{s}}\simeq_{\sigma}\|u\|_{L^{2}_{s}}+\|L^{\theta}u\|_{L^{2}_{s}}\,,\quad\forall u\in\mathcal{D}(L^{\theta})\,.

We can moreover identify domains of
L^{-\theta}
, suitable defined, with
H^{-4\theta}
when
4\theta\leq 5/2
. With this notation, the standard energy estimate
20
20

20

One can prove the estimates also for forcing terms in the Chemin-Lerner spaces
\widetilde{L}^{q}_{t}\mathcal{D}(L^{\theta})_{s}
, which are controlled by
L^{q}_{t}\mathcal{D}(L^{\theta})_{s}
when
q\leq 2
:

(A.42)

\|g\|_{L^{q}_{t}\mathcal{D}(L^{\theta})_{s}}=\|\|(\lambda_{k}^{\theta}g_{k})\|_{\ell^{2}(\mathbb{N})}\|_{L^{q}_{t}}\geq\|(\lambda_{k}^{\theta}\|g_{k}\|_{L^{q}_{t}})\|_{\ell^{2}(\mathbb{N})}=:\|g\|_{\widetilde{L}^{q}_{t}\mathcal{D}(L^{\theta})_{s}}\,.

in
C([0,T];L^{2})\cap L^{2}_{t}\dot{H}^{2}_{s}(I\times(0,T))
for the Dirichlet problem

(A.43)

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+\partial_{s}^{4})u

\displaystyle=g^{(0)}+g^{(1)}+g^{(2)}

\displaystyle u,\partial_{s}u|_{s=0,1}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle u|_{t=0}=u^{\rm in}\in H^{2}_{0}(I)

with

(A.44)

g^{(0)}\in L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{s}\,,\quad g^{(1)}\in L^{4/3}_{t}H^{-1}_{s}\,,\quad g^{(2)}\in L^{2}_{t}H^{-2}_{s}

can be “raised” by simply applying
L^{\theta}
to the equation. The estimate (
A.37
) follows from the choice
\theta=1/4
. ∎

Appendix B
Curvature formulation with forcing

Here we record the full expressions for the rod evolution (
2.33
)-(
2.35
) forced by intrinsic curvature
\zeta_{1}(s,t)
,
\zeta_{2}(s,t)
and twist
\zeta_{3}(s,t)
. Writing out the system as an evolution for
(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\kappa_{3})
highlights the potential backward heat operator in the
\kappa_{3}
evolution and emphasizes the complicated effects of forcing terms.

Recalling the expressions (
2.29
) and (
2.30
), we may calculate

(B.1)

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{s}\cdot\bm{e}_{1}=-(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})_{sss}+(\kappa_{3})_{ss}(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})-\eta\kappa_{2}(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})_{ss}+\mathcal{R}_{\rm a}[\kappa_{j},\zeta_{j},\lambda]

(B.2)

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{s}\cdot\bm{e}_{2}=-(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})_{sss}-(\kappa_{3})_{ss}(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})+\eta\kappa_{1}(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})_{ss}+\mathcal{R}_{\rm b}[\kappa_{j},\zeta_{j},\lambda]

(B.3)

\displaystyle(1+\gamma)\lambda_{ss}-(\kappa_{1}^{2}+\kappa_{2}^{2})\lambda=-\mathcal{R}_{\rm t}[\kappa_{j},\zeta_{j}]\,,

where

(B.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\rm a}

\displaystyle=3\kappa_{3}(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})_{ss}-\eta(\kappa_{2})_{ss}(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})+3(\kappa_{3})_{s}(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})_{s}-2\eta(\kappa_{2})_{s}(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})_{s}

\displaystyle+3\kappa_{3}(\kappa_{3})_{s}(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})+3\kappa_{3}^{2}(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})_{s}-\eta\big(\kappa_{1}\kappa_{3}(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})\big)_{s}-\eta\kappa_{3}\big(\kappa_{1}(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})\big)_{s}

\displaystyle-\kappa_{3}^{3}(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})+\eta\kappa_{2}\kappa_{3}^{2}(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})+(1+\gamma)\big[\kappa_{3}\kappa_{1}^{2}(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})-\kappa_{1}^{2}(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})_{s}

\displaystyle-\kappa_{1}\kappa_{2}(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})_{s}-\kappa_{1}\kappa_{2}\kappa_{3}(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})-\kappa_{1}(\lambda+\kappa_{1}^{2}+\kappa_{2}^{2})_{s}\big]

\displaystyle+\big((\lambda+\kappa_{1}^{2}+\kappa_{2}^{2})\kappa_{1}\big)_{s}-(\lambda+\kappa_{1}^{2}+\kappa_{2}^{2})\kappa_{2}\kappa_{3}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\rm b}

\displaystyle=-3\kappa_{3}(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})_{ss}+\eta(\kappa_{1})_{ss}(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})-3(\kappa_{3})_{s}(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})_{s}+2\eta(\kappa_{1})_{s}(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})_{s}

\displaystyle+3\kappa_{3}(\kappa_{3})_{s}(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})+3\kappa_{3}^{2}(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})_{s}-\eta\big(\kappa_{2}\kappa_{3}(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})\big)_{s}-\eta\kappa_{3}\big(\kappa_{2}(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})\big)_{s}

\displaystyle+\kappa_{3}^{3}(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})-\eta\kappa_{1}\kappa_{3}^{2}(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})+(1+\gamma)\big[-\kappa_{2}\kappa_{1}(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})_{s}-\kappa_{2}^{2}(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})_{s}

\displaystyle+\kappa_{2}\kappa_{1}\kappa_{3}(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})-\kappa_{2}^{2}\kappa_{3}(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})-\kappa_{2}(\lambda+\kappa_{1}^{2}+\kappa_{2}^{2})_{s}\big]

\displaystyle+\big((\lambda+\kappa_{1}^{2}+\kappa_{2}^{2})\kappa_{2}\big)_{s}+(\lambda+\kappa_{1}^{2}+\kappa_{2}^{2})\kappa_{1}\kappa_{3}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\rm t}

\displaystyle=(1+\gamma)\big(-\kappa_{1}(\kappa_{1}+\zeta_{1})_{s}-\kappa_{2}(\kappa_{2}+\zeta_{2})_{s}-\kappa_{3}[\kappa_{1}(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})-\kappa_{2}(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})]\big)_{s}

\displaystyle+(\kappa_{1}^{2}+\kappa_{2}^{2})^{2}+\kappa_{1}(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})_{ss}+\kappa_{2}(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})_{ss}+\eta(\kappa_{1}^{2}+\kappa_{2}^{2})\kappa_{3}(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})

\displaystyle-\eta\big(\kappa_{2}\big(\kappa_{1}(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})\big)_{s}-\kappa_{1}\big(\kappa_{2}(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})\big)_{s}\big)+(\kappa_{3})_{s}\big(\kappa_{2}(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})-\kappa_{1}(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})\big)

\displaystyle+2\kappa_{3}\big(\kappa_{2}(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})_{s}-\kappa_{1}(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})_{s}\big)-\kappa_{3}^{2}\big(\kappa_{1}(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})+\kappa_{2}(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})\big)\,.

Using (
B.1
), (
B.2
), and (
2.32
), we may write out the right-hand side of (
2.8
)-(
2.10
) as

(B.5)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\kappa_{1}

\displaystyle=-(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})_{ssss}+(\kappa_{3})_{sss}(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})-\eta\kappa_{2}(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})_{sss}+\mathcal{R}_{1}[\kappa_{j},\zeta_{j},\lambda]

(B.6)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\kappa_{2}

\displaystyle=-(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})_{ssss}-(\kappa_{3})_{sss}(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})+\eta\kappa_{1}(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})_{sss}+\mathcal{R}_{2}[\kappa_{j},\zeta_{j},\lambda]

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\kappa_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta}{\alpha}(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})_{ss}+\eta(\kappa_{1}^{2}+\kappa_{2}^{2})(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})_{ss}-\big(\kappa_{1}(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})+\kappa_{2}(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})\big)(\kappa_{3})_{ss}

(B.7)

\displaystyle\qquad-\kappa_{1}(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})_{sss}+\kappa_{2}(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})_{sss}+\mathcal{R}_{3}[\kappa_{j},\zeta_{j},\lambda]\,,

with

(B.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}

\displaystyle=\big(\mathcal{R}_{\rm a}\big)_{s}+(\kappa_{3})_{ss}(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})_{s}-\eta\big((\kappa_{2})_{s}+\kappa_{1}\kappa_{3}\big)(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})_{ss}

\displaystyle+\kappa_{3}(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})_{sss}+\kappa_{3}(\kappa_{3})_{ss}(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})-\kappa_{3}\mathcal{R}_{\rm b}+\frac{\eta}{\alpha}\kappa_{2}(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})_{s}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}

\displaystyle=\big(\mathcal{R}_{\rm b}\big)_{s}-(\kappa_{3})_{ss}(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})_{s}-\eta\big(\kappa_{2}\kappa_{3}-(\kappa_{1})_{s}\big)(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})_{ss}

\displaystyle-\kappa_{3}(\kappa_{1}-\zeta_{1})_{sss}+\kappa_{3}(\kappa_{3})_{ss}(\kappa_{2}-\zeta_{2})+\kappa_{3}\mathcal{R}_{\rm a}-\frac{\eta}{\alpha}\kappa_{1}(\kappa_{3}-\zeta_{3})_{s}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{3}

\displaystyle=-\kappa_{2}\mathcal{R}_{\rm a}+\kappa_{1}\mathcal{R}_{\rm b}\,.
